> DEVELOPER

Let's optimize the Approval Administrator Matrix as follows:

1. Separation by Extension Type: Configuration should be distinguished by extension types (e.g., MCP, CLI, and Skill as separate types). On the matrix settings page, users can toggle between different types via buttons to display and configure their respective tables.
2. Consolidated Horizontal Axis: Merge the Company Level and Product Lines into the horizontal axis so they are displayed and configured within a single, unified table.
3. Hierarchical Vertical Axis: Organize the vertical axis by categories with a multi-tiered hierarchy. The first tier is "All", the second tier represents "Macro-categories", and the third tier represents "Micro-categories" (e.g., All $\rightarrow$ System Design $\rightarrow$ Requirements Analysis).
4. Hierarchical Permission Inheritance: Permissions for configuring administrators follow a top-down inheritance rule:Category Hierarchy: 
    - Administrators of the "All" category can assign reviewers for all other sub-categories. Macro-category administrators can assign reviewers for their micro-categories (e.g., the System Design admin can configure the Requirements Analysis admin).
    - Tier Hierarchy: Company-level administrators can configure product-line administrators. For instance, the Company-Level Admin for "All" can assign reviewers for all product lines (e.g., Wireless). The Company-Level Admin for "System Design" can configure System Design reviewers across all product lines (e.g., Wireless).
    - Cross-Dimensional Rule: Similarly, the Wireless Admin for the "All" category has the authority to configure the Wireless System Design admin as well as admins for any other sub-categories within the Wireless product line.

## Approval Administrator Matrix Implementation Report

### 1. Data Model — Drizzle Schema

**File**: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/db/schema/approval.ts`

**Tables**:

1. **`approval_requests`** (21 columns)
   - `id` (text, PK)
   - `extensionId` (text, FK → `extensions.id`, CASCADE on delete)
   - `requestedTier` (enum: `productLine | company`)
   - `subCat` (text) — snapshot of FUNC_TAXONOMY l1 key
   - `productLineId` (text, FK → `productLines.id`, RESTRICT on delete) — required iff `requestedTier='productLine'`
   - `requestedByUserId` (text, FK → `users.id`)
   - `reason`, `status` (enum: `pending | approved | rejected | withdrawn`)
   - `decidedByUserId`, `decidedAt`, `reviewerNote`
   - `createdAt`, `updatedAt`
   - **Indexes**: `idx_approval_status_cell` (status, subCat, requestedTier, productLineId); `idx_approval_ext_status` (extensionId, status)
   - **CHECK**: ensures productLineId is present iff requestedTier='productLine'

2. **`approval_reviewers`** (6 columns)
   - `id` (text, PK)
   - `tier` (enum: `productLine | company`)
   - `subCat` (text) — FUNC_TAXONOMY l1 key
   - `productLineId` (text, FK → `productLines.id`, RESTRICT) — required iff tier='productLine'
   - `userId` (text, FK → `users.id`, CASCADE)
   - `createdAt` (timestamp)
   - **Partial unique indexes**: `approval_reviewers_pl_cell_user_uq` (tier, subCat, productLineId, userId WHERE tier='productLine'); `approval_reviewers_co_cell_user_uq` (tier, subCat, userId WHERE tier='company')
   - **CHECK**: enforces productLineId presence per tier

### 2. Tier Enum

**File**: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/db/schema/extension.ts` (line 51)

**Enum Name**: `officialTierEnum` (PostgreSQL type: `extension_official_tier`)

**Values**: `"productLine" | "company"` (two tiers; no `"unofficial"` — null represents Unofficial)

**On `extensions` table**: column `officialTier` (nullable, owned by approval orchestrator; app code must not write directly)

### 3. Product Lines Table

**File**: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/db/schema/extension.ts` (line 87)

**Table**: `product_lines`

**Columns**:
- `id` (text, PK) — kebab-case identifiers
- `labelEn`, `labelZh` (text, both required) — bilingual display labels
- `sortOrder` (integer, default 0)
- `createdAt` (timestamp, default now())

**Seeded rows** (via migration 0009):
- `wireless` / "Wireless" / "无线" (sort 10)
- `datacom` / "Datacom" / "数通" (sort 20)
- `terminals` / "Terminals" / "终端" (sort 30)
- `cloud` / "Cloud" / "云" (sort 40)

### 4. Function Taxonomy

**File**: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/taxonomy.ts`

**Structure**: Nested array of `FuncTaxonomyNode` objects (not an enum). Three `funcCat` top-level keys, each with 3 `l1` subcategories, each with 3 `l2` leaves. The approval matrix routes on **`l1` keys** only (9 subCats total):

```
workTask: [systemDesign, softDev, testing]
business: [network, embedded, cloud]
tools: [docs, data, vcs]
```

Matrix cells: `9 subCats × 1 company tier + 9 subCats × 4 product lines = 45` cells.

### 5. Server-side Authorization

**File**: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/server/utils/auth.ts`

**Functions**:
- `requireSuperAdmin(event)` — gates matrix UI access; delegates to `isSuperAdmin(db, userId)` in `server/repositories/reviewers.ts`
- `requireCellAdmin(event, cell)` — encodes delegation: super-admins edit everywhere; company-tier admins of subCat X manage productLine cells of subCat X (any productLine); company cells stay super-admin-only

**Role**: `membershipRoleEnum` in `/shared/db/schema/org.ts` has values `"viewer" | "publisher" | "admin" | "superAdmin"`

### 6. ADR-0001 Summary & Recent Updates

**File**: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/docs/adr/0001-official-tier-approval-workflow.md`

**Four locked decisions (2026-06-07)**:
1. Category = `subCat` (FUNC_TAXONOMY l1); 18-cell matrix (9 subCats × 2 tiers)
2. Tiers are independent, not hierarchical
3. `officialTier` is its own enum; `/api/v1` `badge` is read-derived to preserve frozen CLI contract
4. Matrix admin = `superAdmin` membership only

**2026-06-08 update**: Added `productLine` dimension; matrix widens to 45 cells. Company-tier admin of subCat X can now manage productLine cells of subCat X (delegation rule). Partial unique indexes clarify tier-specific uniqueness constraints.

**2026-06-09 update**: Tier revocation implemented. Three new columns on `extensions`: `revoked_at`, `revoked_by_user_id` (FK, ON DELETE SET NULL), `revocation_note`. `applyRevocation` uses CAS via `WHERE official_tier IS NOT NULL`. Inngest event `extension/tier.revoked` fires; notification consumer pending.

## Summary: Approval Administrator Matrix UI

### 1. **Matrix Admin Page**
- **Route path:** `/admin/reviewers` (middleware: `require-auth`, `require-reviewer`)
- **Component file:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/pages/admin/reviewers/index.vue`
- **Props/Composables:** Uses `useFetch` to load:
  - `/api/internal/admin/reviewers` — matrix data + productLines list
  - Passes reviewers, productLines, viewer metadata to `<ReviewerMatrix>` child component
- **Viewer metadata:** `{ isSuperAdmin: boolean, companySubCats: string[] }` — controls per-user edit permissions

### 2. **Matrix Table Components**
Two-tier tabbed layout via `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/components/approvals/ReviewerMatrix.vue`:

**Company Tab** (`ReviewerMatrixCompany.vue`):
- Rows: subCat (from FUNC_TAXONOMY)
- Columns: single "Company Official" tier column
- Each cell displays user chips (name or email) with remove button (if editable)
- Add dialog: email input → lookup user → POST `/api/internal/admin/reviewers/assign`

**Product-Line Tab** (`ReviewerMatrixProductLine.vue`):
- Rows: subCat
- Columns: productLine (sorted by sortOrder)
- Each cell displays reviewers; cells grey out if viewer can't edit
- Add dialog: email input → lookup user → POST `/api/internal/admin/reviewers/assign` with productLineId

### 3. **Extension Types**
**Schema:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/types.ts`
```typescript
export type ExtensionCategory = "skills" | "mcp" | "slash" | "plugins"
```
**Used in:** Extension.category field; labeled in i18n as "skills", "MCP servers", "slash commands", "plugins"

Note: The matrix admin does NOT currently distinguish extension type — it operates on (tier × subCat × [productLineId]) cells regardless of category.

### 4. **Reviewer Assignment UI**
**Component:** Dialog within both `ReviewerMatrixCompany.vue` and `ReviewerMatrixProductLine.vue`
- **Behavior:** Modal dialog triggered by "+ Add" button in each cell
- **User lookup:** Single email input; POST to `/api/internal/admin/users/by-email` (super-admin only)
- **Assignment:** Single-select user per dialog; POST to `/api/internal/admin/reviewers/assign` with `(tier, subCat, [productLineId], userId)`
- **User list source:** All users who have signed in (via email lookup)

### 5. **Internal API Endpoints**
- `GET /api/internal/admin/reviewers` — returns full matrix + productLines + viewer role metadata
- `POST /api/internal/admin/reviewers/assign` — insert reviewer cell (validates cell admin permission server-side)
- `DELETE /api/internal/admin/reviewers/unassign` — remove reviewer by id
- `GET /api/internal/admin/users/by-email` — user lookup for add-reviewer picker (super-admin gated)
- `GET /api/internal/admin/me` — viewer's role + covered cells (used in approvals queue page)

### 6. **i18n Keys**
**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/i18n/locales/en.json`

Matrix-related:
- `admin.reviewers.title` → "Reviewer matrix"
- `admin.reviewers.matrix.subCatColumn` → "Category"
- `admin.reviewers.matrix.tabs.company` → "Company tier"
- `admin.reviewers.matrix.tabs.productLine` → "Product-Line tier"
- `admin.reviewers.matrix.add` → "Add"
- `admin.reviewers.matrix.addTitle` → "Add a reviewer"
- `extensions.officialTier.company` → "Company Official"
- `extensions.officialTier.productLine` → "Product-Line Official"
- `taxonomy.l1.{subCat}` (e.g., `taxonomy.l1.systemDesign` → "System Design")

(Chinese equivalents in `zh.json` at lines 569–591)

## Extension Types, Category Taxonomy, and Approval Matrix - Exploration Report

### 1. Extension Type Enum

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/db/schema/extension.ts` (lines 29-34)

**Enum definition (PostgreSQL):**
```typescript
export const extensionCategoryEnum = pgEnum("extension_category", [
  "skills",
  "mcp",
  "slash",
  "plugins",
]);
```

**Database column:** `extensions.category` (line 100, required, non-nullable)

**TypeScript type:** `ExtensionCategory = "skills" | "mcp" | "slash" | "plugins"` in `/shared/types.ts`

**UI behavior switches:**
- Filter rail: category pills toggle via `/extensions?category=skills` (used in HomeTagRail.vue, TopBar.vue)
- Publish wizard: default category set to "skills" in usePublishWizard composable
- Contract tests: All four values validated in `tests/contract/extensions-list.test.ts`

**Note:** No CLI type in enum — CLAUDE.md mentions "Skills, MCP servers, slash commands, plugins" without CLI being a separate category enum value. Extension type scoping for the redesigned approval matrix **is not yet implemented** (current matrix uses only funcCat/subCat, no extension_category column).

---

### 2. Category Taxonomy Structure

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/taxonomy.ts` (lines 3-28)

**Structure:** 3-tier nested array
- **Tier 1 (funcCat):** `FuncCatKey = "workTask" | "business" | "tools"` 
- **Tier 2 (l1 / subCat):** 9 leaves total (e.g., "systemDesign", "softDev", "testing", "network", "embedded", "cloud", "docs", "data", "vcs")
- **Tier 3 (l2):** 3 entries per l1 (e.g., funcCat="workTask" → l1="softDev" → l2=["frontend", "backend", "devops"])

**Current usage:**
- Filter sidebar (Sidebar.vue): renders nested FUNC_TAXONOMY structure with collapsible l1 leaves and l2 labels
- Publish request form (RequestOfficialDialog.vue): picker for funcCat and subCat (l1 only)
- Approval reviewer matrix (ReviewerMatrixCompany/ProductLine.vue): subCat (l1) only
- Database: stored as text columns `extensions.funcCat`, `extensions.subCat`, `extensions.l2` — l2 is **denormalized storage**, not currently used in approval routing
- Search filter: `shared/search/query.ts` filters by subCat if provided

**Nomenclature note:** The approval schema calls l1 keys "subCat" (in `approval_requests.subCat` and `approval_reviewers.subCat`), following the FUNC_TAXONOMY structure comment.

---

### 3. "All" / Wildcard Handling

**Current state:** NO wildcard/"applies to all" semantics exist in the approval schema.

- `approvalReviewers.subCat` is **always required** (NOT NULL in schema, line 81 of approval.ts)
- `approvalReviewers.productLineId` is conditionally required (iff tier='productLine', enforced by CHECK constraint)
- `approvalRequests.subCat` is also **always required** (NOT NULL, line 33)
- No special sentinel values (e.g., "*", "all") are reserved

**Implications for redesign:**
- To support "reviewer applies to all extension types" or "all subcategories," you'd need to either:
  1. Add a nullable `extensionCategory` column (with NULL = all types)
  2. Introduce sentinel values like "*" or "all" to the subCat column
  3. Create an `approval_reviewer_ext_types` junction table if a reviewer needs multi-type coverage

---

### 4. Permission Inheritance & Hierarchical Authorization

**Primary authorization utility:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/server/utils/auth.ts` (lines 118-132)

**`requireCellAdmin(event, cell)` function:**
- Gate for matrix edits (assign/unassign endpoints)
- Implements ADR-0001 (2026-06-08 addendum) delegation rule:
  - Super-admins can edit any cell
  - Company-tier admins of subCat X can manage **productLine-tier** cells of subCat X only
  - Company-tier cells remain super-admin-only

**Supporting queries in `/server/repositories/reviewers.ts`:**
- `isSuperAdmin(db, userId)` — checks `memberships.role = "superAdmin"` (org-agnostic)
- `isCompanyAdminForSubCat(db, userId, subCat)` — checks if user has `tier='company'` row for that subCat
- `listCompanySubCatsForUser(db, userId)` — returns all company-tier subCats a user manages

**Hierarchical ancestor computation:** NOT YET IMPLEMENTED. Queries are flat (no CTE recursion for l1/l2 hierarchy). To support "company admin for 'softDev' can delegate to 'frontend' reviewers," you'd add a utility to walk the FUNC_TAXONOMY tree from a given leaf upward.

---

### 5. Tests Covering Matrix & Reviewer Logic

**Integration tests:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/tests/integration/repositories/reviewers.test.ts` — insertReviewer, listMatrix, cell lookups (50+ lines)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/tests/integration/repositories/approvals.test.ts` — approval lifecycle with cell-aware reviewer queries (576 lines)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/server/utils/approvals.test.ts` — orchestrator logic, including tier/product-line shape validation (722 lines)

**Component tests:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/components/approvals/ReviewerMatrix.nuxt.test.ts` — matrix tab switching, delegation delegation visual state (224 lines)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/validators/approvals.test.ts` — subCat validator grounded in FUNC_TAXONOMY

**No tests yet exist for:**
- Extension-type scoping (feature not yet added)
- Hierarchical (l1/l2) authorization computations
- Wildcard/"all types" or "all subCats" semantics
```

22	export const approvalRequests = pgTable(
23	  "approval_requests",
24	  {
25	    id: text().primaryKey(),
26	    extensionId: text()
27	      .notNull()
28	      .references(() => extensions.id, { onDelete: "cascade" }),
29	    requestedTier: officialTierEnum().notNull(),
30	    // Snapshot of the functional subCat at submission time. Source of truth is

34	    // Product line snapshot — required iff requestedTier='productLine'.
35	    // RESTRICT on delete keeps the audit trail honest: a product line cannot

37	    productLineId: text().references(() => productLines.id, {
38	      onDelete: "restrict",
39	    }),
40	    requestedByUserId: text()
41	      .notNull()
42	      .references(() => users.id, { onDelete: "cascade" }),
43	    reason: text(),
44	    status: approvalStatusEnum().notNull().default("pending"),
45	    decidedByUserId: text().references(() => users.id, {
46	      onDelete: "set null",
47	    }),
48	    decidedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }),
49	    reviewerNote: text(),
50	    createdAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),
51	    updatedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),
52	  },
53	  (t) => [
54	    // Reviewer queue: pending rows in a given cell, ordered by createdAt.
55	    // productLineId trails so company-tier rows (productLineId IS NULL) still
56	    // collapse onto the same prefix; productLine-tier scans use the full key.
57	    index("idx_approval_status_cell").on(
58	      t.status,
59	      t.subCat,
60	      t.requestedTier,
61	      t.productLineId,
62	    ),
63	    // Publisher "my requests" view and the at-most-one-pending guard.
64	    index("idx_approval_ext_status").on(t.extensionId, t.status),
65	    // Shape invariant: productLineId is present iff requestedTier='productLine'.
66	    check(
67	      "approval_requests_pl_shape_chk",
68	      sql`(requested_tier = 'productLine' AND product_line_id IS NOT NULL)
69	        OR (requested_tier = 'company' AND product_line_id IS NULL)`,
70	    ),
71	  ],
72	);
73	
74	export const approvalReviewers = pgTable(
75	  "approval_reviewers",
76	  {
77	    id: text().primaryKey(),
78	    tier: officialTierEnum().notNull(),
79	    // Same FUNC_TAXONOMY l1 key as approvalRequests.subCat — text rather
80	    // than enum so adding a taxonomy leaf doesn't require a migration.
81	    subCat: text().notNull(),
82	    // Product line this reviewer is scoped to. Required iff tier='productLine',
83	    // null when tier='company'. CHECK enforces the shape; the unique behaviour
84	    // for each tier is expressed below as two partial indexes.
85	    productLineId: text().references(() => productLines.id, {
86	      onDelete: "restrict",
87	    }),
88	    userId: text()
89	      .notNull()
90	      .references(() => users.id, { onDelete: "cascade" }),
91	    createdAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),
92	  },
93	  (t) => [
94	    // Tier-aware uniqueness. ProductLine cells include productLineId in the
95	    // key; company cells do not. Two partial indexes are clearer than relying
96	    // on NULLS NOT DISTINCT and let each tier's arity stand on its own.
97	    uniqueIndex("approval_reviewers_pl_cell_user_uq")
98	      .on(t.tier, t.subCat, t.productLineId, t.userId)
99	      .where(sql`tier = 'productLine'`),
100	    uniqueIndex("approval_reviewers_co_cell_user_uq")
101	      .on(t.tier, t.subCat, t.userId)
102	      .where(sql`tier = 'company'`),
103	    index("idx_approval_reviewers_cell").on(
104	      t.tier,
105	      t.subCat,
106	      t.productLineId,
107	    ),
108	    // Shape invariant: productLineId is present iff tier='productLine'.
109	    check(
110	      "approval_reviewers_pl_shape_chk",
111	      sql`(tier = 'productLine' AND product_line_id IS NOT NULL)
112	        OR (tier = 'company' AND product_line_id IS NULL)`,
113	    ),
114	  ],
115	);
116

1	import { betterAuth } from "better-auth"
2	import { drizzleAdapter } from "better-auth/adapters/drizzle"
3	import { createError, getRequestHeaders, type H3Event } from "h3"
4	
5	import type { OfficialTier } from "~~/shared/approvals/state"
6	
7	import {
8	  isCompanyAdminForSubCat,
9	  isSuperAdmin,
10	} from "~~/server/repositories/reviewers"
11	import {
12	  accounts,
13	  sessions,
14	  users,
15	  verifications,
16	} from "~~/shared/db/schema/auth"
17	
18	import { useDb } from "./db"
19	
20	function makeAuth() {
21	  const db = useDb()
22	  return betterAuth({
23	    database: drizzleAdapter(db, {
24	      provider: "pg",
25	      schema: {
26	        user: users,
27	        session: sessions,
28	        account: accounts,
29	        verification: verifications,
30	      },
31	    }),
32	    emailAndPassword: { enabled: true },
33	    user: {
34	      additionalFields: {
35	        locale: {
36	          type: "string",
37	          required: false,
38	          defaultValue: "en",
39	          input: false,
40	        },
41	        themePreference: {
42	          type: "string",
43	          required: false,
44	          defaultValue: "ivory",
45	          input: false,
46	        },
47	        defaultDeptId: {
48	          type: "string",
49	          required: false,
50	          input: false,
51	        },
52	      },
53	    },
54	  })
55	}
56	
57	type AuthInstance = ReturnType<typeof makeAuth>
58	
59	let _auth: AuthInstance | undefined
60	
61	export function useAuthServer(): AuthInstance {
62	  if (!_auth) _auth = makeAuth()
63	  return _auth
64	}
65	
66	type SessionPayload = Awaited<
67	  ReturnType<AuthInstance["api"]["getSession"]>
68	>
69	export type SessionUser = NonNullable<SessionPayload>["user"]
70	
71	function toFetchHeaders(raw: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>): Headers {
72	  const headers = new Headers()
73	  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(raw)) {
74	    if (value === undefined) continue
75	    headers.append(key, Array.isArray(value) ? value.join(", ") : value)
76	  }
77	  return headers
78	}
79	
80	export async function getSessionUser(event: H3Event): Promise<SessionUser | null> {
81	  const auth = useAuthServer()
82	  const session = await auth.api.getSession({
83	    headers: toFetchHeaders(getRequestHeaders(event)),
84	  })
85	  return session?.user ?? null
86	}
87	
88	export async function requireUser(event: H3Event): Promise<SessionUser> {
89	  const user = await getSessionUser(event)
90	  if (!user) {
91	    throw createError({ statusCode: 401, statusMessage: "Unauthenticated" })
92	  }
93	  return user
94	}
95	
96	// Super-admin gate for the reviewer-matrix admin surface. Returns the
97	// session user when they hold any membership with role='superAdmin';
98	// throws 401 when unauthenticated and 403 otherwise. The membership
99	// lookup goes through the reviewers repo so the policy lives in one
100	// place — see `server/repositories/reviewers.ts`.
101	export async function requireSuperAdmin(event: H3Event): Promise<SessionUser> {
102	  const user = await requireUser(event)
103	  if (!(await isSuperAdmin(useDb(), user.id))) {
104	    throw createError({ statusCode: 403, statusMessage: "Forbidden" })
105	  }
106	  return user
107	}
108	
109	// Cell-aware authorisation for matrix edits. Encodes the delegation rule
110	// from ADR-0001 (2026-06-08 addendum):
111	//   - super-admins can edit every cell;
112	//   - company-tier admins of subCat X may manage productLine cells of
113	//     subCat X (any productLine);
114	//   - company-tier cells stay super-admin-only.
115	// Callers pass the cell coordinates explicitly — for `unassign`, load the
116	// target row first so the gate authorises against that row's coordinates
117	// rather than the caller's request body.
118	export async function requireCellAdmin(
119	  event: H3Event,
120	  cell: { tier: OfficialTier; subCat: string; productLineId: string | null },
121	): Promise<SessionUser> {
122	  const user = await requireUser(event)
123	  const db = useDb()
124	  if (await isSuperAdmin(db, user.id)) return user
125	  if (
126	    cell.tier === "productLine" &&
127	    (await isCompanyAdminForSubCat(db, user.id, cell.subCat))
128	  ) {
129	    return user
130	  }
131	  throw createError({ statusCode: 403, statusMessage: "not_authorized" })
132	}
133

1	<script setup lang="ts">
2	// Tabbed shell over the two tier-specific matrix views.
3	// Company tab — (subCat × admin). Super-admin-only edits.
4	// Product-Line tab — (subCat × productLine) grid. Edits permitted for
5	//   super-admins anywhere and for company admins of the row's subCat
6	//   (delegation rule from ADR-0001, 2026-06-08 addendum).
7	// The active tab persists in the URL hash so deep links survive a reload.
8	
9	type Tab = "company" | "productLine"
10	
11	export interface ReviewerRow {
12	  id: string
13	  tier: Tab
14	  subCat: string
15	  productLineId: string | null
16	  userId: string
17	  userEmail: string
18	  userName: string | null
19	  canEdit: boolean
20	}
21	
22	export interface ProductLine {
23	  id: string
24	  labelEn: string
25	  labelZh: string
26	  sortOrder: number
27	}
28	
29	export interface Viewer {
30	  isSuperAdmin: boolean
31	  companySubCats: string[]
32	}
33	
34	const props = defineProps<{
35	  reviewers: ReviewerRow[]
36	  productLines: ProductLine[]
37	  viewer: Viewer
38	}>()
39	const emit = defineEmits<{ (e: "refresh"): void }>()
40	
41	const { t } = useI18n()
42	
43	const route = useRoute()
44	const router = useRouter()
45	
46	function hashToTab(hash: string): Tab {
47	  return hash === "#tab=productLine" ? "productLine" : "company"
48	}
49	
50	const activeTab = ref<Tab>(hashToTab(route.hash))
51	
52	watch(activeTab, (next) => {
53	  router.replace({ hash: `#tab=${next}` })
54	})
55	
56	// Reverse binding so browser back / forward (or any external hash change)
57	// keeps the UI in sync with the URL. Guard against the loop the assignment
58	// would otherwise trigger via the watcher above.
59	watch(
60	  () => route.hash,
61	  (next) => {
62	    const tab = hashToTab(next)
63	    if (activeTab.value !== tab) activeTab.value = tab
64	  },
65	)
66	
67	// Provide a Ref<boolean> for the subviews so they don't need to thread
68	// the viewer prop themselves. Wrapped as a computed so reactivity follows
69	// the prop without an explicit re-broadcast.
70	const viewerIsSuperAdmin = computed(() => props.viewer.isSuperAdmin)
71	provide("viewerIsSuperAdmin", viewerIsSuperAdmin)
72	
73	const companyRows = computed(() =>
74	  props.reviewers
75	    .filter((r) => r.tier === "company")
76	    .map((r) => ({
77	      id: r.id,
78	      subCat: r.subCat,
79	      userId: r.userId,
80	      userEmail: r.userEmail,
81	      userName: r.userName,
82	      canEdit: r.canEdit,
83	    })),
84	)
85	
86	const productLineRows = computed(() =>
87	  props.reviewers
88	    .filter((r): r is ReviewerRow & { productLineId: string } =>
89	      r.tier === "productLine" && r.productLineId !== null,
90	    )
91	    .map((r) => ({
92	      id: r.id,
93	      subCat: r.subCat,
94	      productLineId: r.productLineId,
95	      userId: r.userId,
96	      userEmail: r.userEmail,
97	      userName: r.userName,
98	      canEdit: r.canEdit,
99	    })),
100	)
101	</script>
102	
103	<template>
104	  <div class="space-y-4">
105	    <div
106	      role="tablist"
107	      class="inline-flex items-center gap-1 rounded-lg border border-(--color-border) bg-(--color-card) p-1"
108	    >
109	      <button
110	        type="button"
111	        role="tab"
112	        :aria-selected="activeTab === 'company'"
113	        class="rounded-md px-3 py-1.5 text-sm font-medium"
114	        :class="
115	          activeTab === 'company'
116	            ? 'bg-(--color-bg) text-(--color-ink) shadow-sm'
117	            : 'text-(--color-ink-muted) hover:text-(--color-ink)'
118	        "
119	        @click="activeTab = 'company'"
120	      >
121	        {{ t("admin.reviewers.matrix.tabs.company") }}
122	      </button>
123	      <button
124	        type="button"
125	        role="tab"
126	        :aria-selected="activeTab === 'productLine'"
127	        class="rounded-md px-3 py-1.5 text-sm font-medium"
128	        :class="
129	          activeTab === 'productLine'
130	            ? 'bg-(--color-bg) text-(--color-ink) shadow-sm'
131	            : 'text-(--color-ink-muted) hover:text-(--color-ink)'
132	        "
133	        @click="activeTab = 'productLine'"
134	      >
135	        {{ t("admin.reviewers.matrix.tabs.productLine") }}
136	      </button>
137	    </div>
138	
139	    <p
140	      v-if="!viewer.isSuperAdmin"
141	      class="text-xs text-(--color-ink-muted)"
142	    >
143	      {{ t("admin.reviewers.matrix.delegationNote") }}
144	    </p>
145	
146	    <ReviewerMatrixCompany

148	      :reviewers="companyRows"
149	      @refresh="emit('refresh')"
150	    />
151	    <ReviewerMatrixProductLine
152	      v-else

155	      :viewer-company-sub-cats="viewer.companySubCats"
156	      @refresh="emit('refresh')"
157	    />
158	  </div>
159	</template>

1	import { and, asc, eq, isNull } from "drizzle-orm"
2	
3	import type { OfficialTier } from "~~/shared/approvals/state"
4	import {
5	  approvalReviewers,
6	  memberships,
7	  users,
8	} from "~~/shared/db/schema"
9	
10	import type { Transactable } from "./types"
11	
12	// `approval_reviewers` table accessor. Backs the reviewer matrix:
13	// (tier × subCat × productLineId?) → list of users assigned to decide on
14	// that cell. ProductLineId participates only for the `productLine` tier;
15	// the DB CHECK constraint enforces the iff-rule. Also exposes the
16	// super-admin lookup and the company-admin-for-subCat helper used by the
17	// delegated matrix-edit gate.
18	
19	export type { OfficialTier }
20	
21	export interface ReviewerRow {
22	  id: string
23	  tier: OfficialTier
24	  subCat: string
25	  productLineId: string | null
26	  userId: string
27	  userEmail: string
28	  userName: string | null
29	  createdAt: Date
30	}
31	
32	export interface InsertReviewerRow {
33	  id: string
34	  tier: OfficialTier
35	  subCat: string
36	  productLineId: string | null
37	  userId: string
38	}
39	
40	export interface ReviewerCell {
41	  tier: OfficialTier
42	  subCat: string
43	  productLineId: string | null
44	}
45	
46	// Full matrix view, joined with `users` so the matrix UI can render
47	// reviewer chips with a recognisable label without an extra round-trip.
48	// productLineId sorts NULLS FIRST so company-tier rows precede the
49	// productLine grid in iteration order.
50	export async function listMatrix(db: Transactable): Promise<ReviewerRow[]> {
51	  return db
52	    .select({
53	      id: approvalReviewers.id,
54	      tier: approvalReviewers.tier,
55	      subCat: approvalReviewers.subCat,
56	      productLineId: approvalReviewers.productLineId,
57	      userId: approvalReviewers.userId,
58	      userEmail: users.email,
59	      userName: users.name,
60	      createdAt: approvalReviewers.createdAt,
61	    })
62	    .from(approvalReviewers)
63	    .innerJoin(users, eq(users.id, approvalReviewers.userId))
64	    .orderBy(
65	      asc(approvalReviewers.tier),
66	      asc(approvalReviewers.subCat),
67	      asc(approvalReviewers.productLineId),
68	      asc(users.email),
69	    )
70	}
71	
72	// Cells (tier × subCat × productLineId?) where the given user is assigned.
73	// Used by the orchestrator to drive `listPendingForCells` from `approvals.ts`.
74	export async function listCellsForUser(
75	  db: Transactable,
76	  userId: string,
77	): Promise<ReviewerCell[]> {
78	  return db
79	    .select({
80	      tier: approvalReviewers.tier,
81	      subCat: approvalReviewers.subCat,
82	      productLineId: approvalReviewers.productLineId,
83	    })
84	    .from(approvalReviewers)
85	    .where(eq(approvalReviewers.userId, userId))
86	}
87	
88	// SubCats this user is a company-tier reviewer for. Drives the delegated
89	// matrix-edit gate: a company admin of subCat X can manage productLine
90	// reviewers for subCat X (and only X).
91	export async function listCompanySubCatsForUser(
92	  db: Transactable,
93	  userId: string,
94	): Promise<string[]> {
95	  const rows = await db
96	    .select({ subCat: approvalReviewers.subCat })
97	    .from(approvalReviewers)
98	    .where(
99	      and(
100	        eq(approvalReviewers.userId, userId),
101	        eq(approvalReviewers.tier, "company"),
102	      ),
103	    )
104	  return rows.map((r) => r.subCat)
105	}
106	
107	// Cheap per-cell version of the above for endpoint authorisation paths.
108	export async function isCompanyAdminForSubCat(
109	  db: Transactable,
110	  userId: string,
111	  subCat: string,
112	): Promise<boolean> {
113	  const [row] = await db
114	    .select({ id: approvalReviewers.id })
115	    .from(approvalReviewers)
116	    .where(
117	      and(
118	        eq(approvalReviewers.userId, userId),
119	        eq(approvalReviewers.tier, "company"),
120	        eq(approvalReviewers.subCat, subCat),
121	      ),
122	    )
123	    .limit(1)
124	  return !!row
125	}
126	
127	// Per-cell reviewer list — useful for the orchestrator's notification fan-out.
128	// For company tier the productLineId argument is ignored (and asserted null
129	// in callers); for productLine tier it must be supplied or the cell match is
130	// ambiguous.
131	export async function findReviewersFor(
132	  db: Transactable,
133	  tier: OfficialTier,
134	  subCat: string,
135	  productLineId: string | null,
136	): Promise<string[]> {
137	  const rows = await db
138	    .select({ userId: approvalReviewers.userId })
139	    .from(approvalReviewers)
140	    .where(
141	      and(
142	        eq(approvalReviewers.tier, tier),
143	        eq(approvalReviewers.subCat, subCat),
144	        productLineId === null
145	          ? isNull(approvalReviewers.productLineId)
146	          : eq(approvalReviewers.productLineId, productLineId),
147	      ),
148	    )
149	  return rows.map((r) => r.userId)
150	}
151	
152	export async function insertReviewer(
153	  db: Transactable,
154	  row: InsertReviewerRow,
155	): Promise<void> {
156	  // No `target` on the conflict clause — the table now carries two partial
157	  // unique indexes (one per tier) and Drizzle can't synthesise a typed
158	  // target across both. The CHECK + partial uniques still de-dupe at the
159	  // DB layer; this just makes a duplicate insert a no-op instead of an error.
160	  await db.insert(approvalReviewers).values(row).onConflictDoNothing()
161	}
162	
163	export async function deleteReviewer(
164	  db: Transactable,
165	  id: string,
166	): Promise<void> {
167	  await db.delete(approvalReviewers).where(eq(approvalReviewers.id, id))
168	}
169	
170	// Single-row fetch used by the unassign endpoint so it can authorise
171	// against the row's tier/subCat (delegation rule) before deleting.
172	export async function findReviewerById(
173	  db: Transactable,
174	  id: string,
175	): Promise<ReviewerRow | null> {
176	  const [row] = await db
177	    .select({
178	      id: approvalReviewers.id,
179	      tier: approvalReviewers.tier,
180	      subCat: approvalReviewers.subCat,
181	      productLineId: approvalReviewers.productLineId,
182	      userId: approvalReviewers.userId,
183	      userEmail: users.email,
184	      userName: users.name,
185	      createdAt: approvalReviewers.createdAt,
186	    })
187	    .from(approvalReviewers)
188	    .innerJoin(users, eq(users.id, approvalReviewers.userId))
189	    .where(eq(approvalReviewers.id, id))
190	    .limit(1)
191	  return row ?? null
192	}
193	
194	// Super-admin check. A user is a super-admin if any of their memberships
195	// carries the `superAdmin` role. Decoupled from a specific org so seeded
196	// super-admins keep their authority even when the default org is renamed.
197	export async function isSuperAdmin(
198	  db: Transactable,
199	  userId: string,
200	): Promise<boolean> {
201	  const [row] = await db
202	    .select({ id: memberships.id })
203	    .from(memberships)
204	    .where(
205	      and(
206	        eq(memberships.userId, userId),
207	        eq(memberships.role, "superAdmin"),
208	      ),
209	    )
210	    .limit(1)
211	  return !!row
212	}
213

# Approval Administrator Matrix — Implementation Plan

## 1. Schema

Replace `approval_reviewers` with `approval_admins` (rename rationale below). New table:

```
approval_admins(
  id                  text PK,
  extension_category  extension_category NOT NULL,           -- reuse existing enum
  tier                extension_official_tier NOT NULL,      -- 'company' | 'productLine'
  product_line_id     text REFERENCES product_lines ON DELETE RESTRICT,
  category_level      admin_category_level NOT NULL,         -- NEW enum: 'all' | 'macro' | 'micro'
  category_key        text NOT NULL,                         -- '*' | l1 key | l2 key
  user_id             text NOT NULL REFERENCES users ON DELETE CASCADE,
  created_at          timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
)
```

New enum `admin_category_level` ∈ {`all`, `macro`, `micro`}.

**CHECK constraints** (mirror the iff pattern from `shared/db/schema/approval.ts`):
- Column-tier shape (same as today): `(tier='productLine' AND product_line_id IS NOT NULL) OR (tier='company' AND product_line_id IS NULL)`.
- Category-level shape: `(category_level='all' AND category_key='*') OR (category_level='macro' AND category_key <> '*') OR (category_level='micro' AND category_key <> '*')`. Membership of the key in FUNC_TAXONOMY l1/l2 is enforced at the validator layer (Zod) — same pattern `SUB_CAT_KEY_LIST` uses today — so adding a taxonomy leaf stays a code-only change.

**Partial unique indexes** (one per tier-column shape, matching the today/yesterday pattern):
- `approval_admins_pl_uq` on `(extension_category, tier, product_line_id, category_level, category_key, user_id) WHERE tier='productLine'`.
- `approval_admins_co_uq` on `(extension_category, tier, category_level, category_key, user_id) WHERE tier='company'`.

**Routing indexes**:
- `idx_admins_cell` on `(extension_category, tier, product_line_id, category_level, category_key)` — drives the fan-out queue scan and `findAdminsFor`.
- `idx_admins_user` on `(user_id)` — drives `listCellsForUser` and the matrix-edit gate's "is this user a covering admin?" lookup.

**`approval_requests` columns**: add `extension_category extension_category NOT NULL` and `l2 text` (nullable — see edge case b). **Argue for snapshotting**: routing fan-out reads these on every queue scan; joining `extensions` every time costs an extra index lookup for a value that the publisher's submission semantically froze in time. Snapshot matches the existing `subCat`/`requestedTier` precedent on the same table. Add `idx_approval_status_cell_v2` on `(status, extension_category, requested_tier, product_line_id, sub_cat, l2)` to keep the queue scan tight.

## 2. Naming: rename to `approval_admins`

Today's term "reviewer" reads as "user who decides a request". With the redesign, a row in the matrix table grants **both** admin authority (configure descendant cells in their fan-out shadow) **and** the duty to decide requests routed there. "Admin" is the more accurate noun; "reviewer" survives in `decided_by_user_id` / `reviewer_note` on `approval_requests` (the act of deciding), where it belongs. The orchestrator's `findReviewersFor` becomes `findAdminsFor`; `isReviewerForCell` becomes `isAdminCovering`. The Vue component `ReviewerQueueTable.vue` keeps its name — the queue is reviewers reviewing.

## 3. Taxonomy helpers in `shared/taxonomy.ts`

```ts
export function l2KeysFor(l1: string): string[]                 // ['reqAnalysis', ...]
export function l1KeyFor(l2: string): string | null             // 'systemDesign'
export function isL1Key(k: string): boolean
export function isL2Key(k: string): boolean
export const ALL_L1_KEYS: readonly string[]                     // 9 entries
export const ALL_L2_KEYS: readonly string[]                     // 27 entries
// Covers-walk for the authz gate:
export function categoryAncestors(level: 'all'|'macro'|'micro', key: string):
  Array<{level: 'all'|'macro'|'micro', key: string}>            // self + all ancestors up to '*'
```

Build the l1→l2 and l2→l1 maps at module load (one pass over `FUNC_TAXONOMY`), same way `SUB_CAT_KEYS` is built today.

## 4. Authorization rewrite — `requireCellAdmin`

New 5-coordinate signature:
```ts
requireCellAdmin(event, cell: {
  extensionCategory: ExtensionCategory,
  tier: OfficialTier,
  productLineId: string | null,
  categoryLevel: 'all'|'macro'|'micro',
  categoryKey: string,
}): Promise<SessionUser>
```

Logic (`server/utils/auth.ts`):
1. `requireUser` → if `isSuperAdmin` → return.
2. Compute the **covering set** for the target cell: column ancestors `[(company, null)]` if target tier=`productLine`, plus self; category ancestors via `categoryAncestors(level, key)`.
3. Query `approval_admins` for any row of this `user_id` and `extension_category` whose `(tier, product_line_id)` is in the column-ancestor set AND `(category_level, category_key)` is in the category-ancestor set. Repo helper: `findCoveringAdmin(db, userId, cell)` — single index probe on `idx_admins_user`.
4. Found → return; else 403 `not_authorized`.

**Call-sites to update**:
- `server/api/internal/admin/reviewers/assign.post.ts` — read 5 coords from body.
- `server/api/internal/admin/reviewers/unassign.delete.ts` — `findAdminById` loads all 5; gate against the row's own coords (same defense as today).
- `server/api/internal/admin/reviewers/index.get.ts` — replace the company-subCat list with `listCoveringCellsForUser(userId)` and annotate each matrix cell with `canEdit` by intersecting against the covering set.
- `server/utils/approvals.ts` decide path — `isReviewerForCell` becomes `isAdminCovering` with all 5 coords drawn from `approval_requests`.

## 5. Routing fan-out (queue scan)

For a request `(E, T, C, l1=X, l2=Y)`, the queue duty falls on any admin whose row covers it on **both** dims. Build the candidate cell set in code (cheap — at most 3 category rows × 2 column rows = 6 combos) and OR them in one SQL:

```ts
// pseudo-Drizzle
const colShapes = T === 'productLine'
  ? [{tier:'productLine', plId:C}, {tier:'company', plId:null}]
  : [{tier:'company', plId:null}]
const catShapes = [
  {level:'all',   key:'*'},
  {level:'macro', key:X},
  ...(Y ? [{level:'micro', key:Y}] : []),     // edge case b
]
const ors = colShapes.flatMap(col => catShapes.map(cat =>
  and(eq(extension_category, E),
      eq(tier, col.tier), col.plId === null ? isNull(...) : eq(product_line_id, col.plId),
      eq(category_level, cat.level), eq(category_key, cat.key))))
```

Symmetric query for the listing direction (`listPendingForCells`): replace the 3-tuple with a 5-tuple in `server/repositories/approvals.ts:listPendingForCells`, and union-expand each request's cell into its covering set before computing the candidate `approval_requests` clauses.

**No-l2 request**: drop the micro row from `catShapes`. A `micro=Y` admin never matches a request without `l2`, by construction — the row is unreachable except through `all=*` or `macro=l1`, which is the right semantic.

## 6. UI restructure

Delete `ReviewerMatrixCompany.vue` and `ReviewerMatrixProductLine.vue`. `ReviewerMatrix.vue` becomes one unified table:

- **Top control**: segmented control over `extension_category` (4 buttons: skills / mcp / slash / plugins). Persist in URL hash like the today's `#tab=` does (`#cat=skills`).
- **Columns** (left → right): `All categories`(meta) | `Company` | `Wireless` | `Datacom` | `Terminals` | `Cloud`. The first "meta" column is dropped — there is no per-extension-category "All categories" column; the column axis is column-tier only.
- **Rows**: 3-tier hierarchy rendered as **indented collapsible rows**:
  - Sticky top row `All (*)` (always visible; one row).
  - 9 macro rows (collapsible group headers). Each expands to its 3 micro children.
  - Each cell = chips for admins assigned to **that exact (level, key, tier, productLine)**. Inherited coverage is rendered as a faint badge on descendant cells ("inherits 2 from softDev") so the operator sees the effective set without losing the explicit-vs-inherited distinction.
- Per-cell `canEdit` comes from the API annotation (same shape as today, just with all 5 coords).

`ReviewerMatrix.nuxt.test.ts` rewrites: assert single table, category toggle, collapsible macros, inheritance badges.

## 7. Internal API contract

`/api/internal/admin/reviewers/index.get` — response widens:
```ts
{
  ok: true,
  productLines: [...],
  admins: Array<{
    id, extensionCategory, tier, productLineId,
    categoryLevel, categoryKey, userId, userEmail, userName, canEdit
  }>,
  viewer: { isSuperAdmin: boolean, coveringCells: Array<{...5 coords}> }
}
```
Rename the path eventually; keep `/reviewers/` for v1 to avoid touching the SPA fetcher.

`assign.post` and `unassign.delete` payloads gain `extensionCategory`, `categoryLevel`, `categoryKey`. `AssignReviewerSchema` in `shared/validators/approvals.ts` gains the matching Zod fields and a refine that checks `categoryKey` membership in the right set (per `categoryLevel`).

## 8. Edge cases / risks

- **(a) in-flight `approval_requests` during clean drop+recreate**: ADR-0001's tier-revocation update plus the user's "pre-launch, seed data only" stance means a destructive migration is OK. Drop `approval_admins`/`approval_reviewers` and **also drop+recreate `approval_requests`** so the new NOT NULL `extension_category` doesn't need a backfill story. Seed script in `scripts/` recreates the 4 super-admin admins.
- **(b) extensions without `l2`**: snapshot `l2=NULL` onto the request; routing fan-out simply omits the micro candidate. No special-case branch in the SQL.
- **(c) duplicate notifications when admin matches at both macro and micro**: fan-out is over `user_id` not row — `findAdminsFor` returns `DISTINCT user_id`. Same dedup pattern as today's `listReviewerQueue` Set.
- **(d) i18n for 27 l2 keys**: extend the existing `taxonomy.l2.<key>` namespace already used by `SubCatPicker.vue`; add 27 EN + 27 ZH entries. Same file as the l1 keys live in.
- **(e) tests**: rewrite `shared/validators/approvals.test.ts` (new fields + refines), `shared/taxonomy.test.ts` (new helpers), `app/components/approvals/ReviewerMatrix.nuxt.test.ts`, integration tests in `tests/integration/approvals/*` against the new routing fan-out. The pure-state `shared/approvals/state.ts` doesn't need to change.

## 9. Verification

```
bun run drizzle:generate          # one new migration file
bun run db:reset && bun run db:seed
bun run typecheck && bun run lint
bun run test                       # unit + nuxt-component
bun run test:integration           # repository + routing fan-out
bun run dev                        # smoke
```

Manual smoke (super-admin account):
1. Open `/admin/reviewers`, toggle through all 4 extension categories.
2. Assign `(skills, productLine, wireless, macro, softDev)` to user A.
3. Submit an extension request as a publisher with `extensionCategory=skills`, `subCat=softDev`, `l2=backend`, `requestedTier=productLine`, `productLineId=wireless` → confirm user A's queue picks it up.
4. Switch to a non-super company-admin of `(skills, company, all, *)` → confirm they can edit any descendant skills cell but cannot touch `mcp`/`slash`/`plugins`.
5. Revoke (per ADR-0001 §Update 2026-06-09) still works untouched — the orchestrator path isn't entangled.

### Critical Files for Implementation
- /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/db/schema/approval.ts
- /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/taxonomy.ts
- /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/server/utils/auth.ts
- /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/server/repositories/reviewers.ts
- /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/components/approvals/ReviewerMatrix.vue
```

## Approved Plan:
# Optimize the Approval Administrator Matrix

## Context

The Approval Administrator Matrix today (per ADR-0001 + `shared/db/schema/approval.ts`) is a 2-tab UI over `(tier × subCat × productLineId?)` cells — 45 cells per the company/PL split, with exact-match routing and a flat one-level category axis (FUNC_TAXONOMY l1 only). Reviewer authority over the PL tab is a one-off delegation written into `requireCellAdmin`.

The change collapses the two tabs into one table, adds an extension-category axis (toggled at the top), and rebuilds the vertical axis as a 3-tier `All → Macro (l1) → Micro (l2)` hierarchy. Permission inheritance becomes a 2-dimensional cover relation — admins at a higher-or-equal cell on **either** axis can configure descendant cells. Routing for an approval request fans out so that an `All × Wireless` admin sees every Wireless request without needing a row per micro-category.

User decisions (confirmed):
1. Extension types: existing 4 — `skills | mcp | slash | plugins` (no schema change to the enum).
2. Routing: fan-out — `l2 cell ∪ macro cell ∪ All cell` for the matching column.
3. Migration: clean drop+recreate (product is pre-launch; current rows are seed data).

## Approach

Rename `approval_reviewers` → `approval_admins`. A row in this table grants **both** admin authority over its covered shadow **and** the duty to decide requests routed into that shadow — "admin" is the more accurate noun. The word "reviewer" stays on `approval_requests.decided_by_user_id` / `reviewer_note` where it refers to the act of deciding a specific request.

## Schema (new migration)

New enum `admin_category_level = ('all','macro','micro')`.

`approval_admins`:
- `id text PK`
- `extension_category extension_category NOT NULL` (reuse existing enum)
- `tier extension_official_tier NOT NULL` ('company' | 'productLine')
- `product_line_id text REFERENCES product_lines ON DELETE RESTRICT` (null iff tier=company)
- `category_level admin_category_level NOT NULL`
- `category_key text NOT NULL` ('*' iff level='all'; else l1 key or l2 key)
- `user_id text NOT NULL REFERENCES users ON DELETE CASCADE`
- `created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`

CHECK constraints (mirror the existing iff-pattern in `shared/db/schema/approval.ts`):
- Column-tier shape: same as today's `approval_reviewers_pl_shape_chk`.
- Category-level shape: `(level='all' AND key='*') OR (level<>'all' AND key<>'*')`. Membership in FUNC_TAXONOMY stays a validator-layer concern so a taxonomy edit doesn't trigger a migration — matches today's `SUB_CAT_KEY_LIST` pattern.

Partial unique indexes (one per tier, like today):
- `approval_admins_pl_uq` on `(extension_category, tier, product_line_id, category_level, category_key, user_id) WHERE tier='productLine'`.
- `approval_admins_co_uq` on `(extension_category, tier, category_level, category_key, user_id) WHERE tier='company'`.

Routing/scan indexes:
- `idx_admins_cell` on `(extension_category, tier, product_line_id, category_level, category_key)` — drives the queue fan-out.
- `idx_admins_user` on `(user_id)` — drives the `requireCellAdmin` covering-row probe.

`approval_requests` — drop & recreate with two added NOT NULL/nullable snapshot columns:
- `extension_category extension_category NOT NULL`
- `l2 text` (nullable — see edge case below)

Snapshotting both (rather than joining `extensions`) matches the existing `sub_cat`/`requested_tier` precedent on the same table and keeps the queue scan a single index probe. Replace today's `idx_approval_status_cell` with one that includes `extension_category` and `l2`.

## Taxonomy helpers — `shared/taxonomy.ts`

```ts
export function l2KeysFor(l1: string): string[]
export function l1KeyFor(l2: string): string | null
export function isL1Key(k: string): boolean
export function isL2Key(k: string): boolean
export const ALL_L1_KEYS: readonly string[]
export const ALL_L2_KEYS: readonly string[]
export function categoryAncestors(
  level: 'all'|'macro'|'micro', key: string,
): Array<{ level: 'all'|'macro'|'micro', key: string }>   // self + ancestors up to (all,'*')
```

Maps built once at module load by walking `FUNC_TAXONOMY` — same shape as the existing `SUB_CAT_KEYS` derivation.

## Authorization — `server/utils/auth.ts`

```ts
requireCellAdmin(event, cell: {
  extensionCategory: ExtensionCategory,
  tier: OfficialTier,
  productLineId: string | null,
  categoryLevel: 'all'|'macro'|'micro',
  categoryKey: string,
}): Promise<SessionUser>
```

Logic:
1. `requireUser` → `isSuperAdmin` → return.
2. Column-axis ancestors: `[(company, null)]` if target tier='productLine', plus self; just self if target is company.
3. Category-axis ancestors: `categoryAncestors(level, key)`.
4. New repo helper `findCoveringAdmin(db, userId, cell)` runs **one** query on `idx_admins_user` filtered to the at-most-6 candidate cells (≤2 column shapes × ≤3 category shapes). Found → return; else 403.

Cross-extensionCategory authority is intentionally absent — each ext-type matrix is independent (per the user's "Separation by Extension Type" rule).

## Routing fan-out — `server/repositories/reviewers.ts` → `admins.ts`

`findAdminsFor(db, request)` where `request = {extensionCategory, tier, productLineId, subCat, l2 | null}`:

```ts
const colShapes = request.tier === 'productLine'
  ? [{tier:'productLine', plId:request.productLineId}, {tier:'company', plId:null}]
  : [{tier:'company', plId:null}]
const catShapes = [
  {level:'all',   key:'*'},
  {level:'macro', key:request.subCat},
  ...(request.l2 ? [{level:'micro', key:request.l2}] : []),
]
// OR over the cartesian product (≤6 rows), filtered by extensionCategory.
// SELECT DISTINCT user_id … — same dedup pattern as today's listReviewerQueue Set.
```

Symmetric expansion in `listPendingForCells` (used by `/api/internal/admin/me`'s queue annotation): expand each of the viewer's covering cells into the matching request candidates before composing the SQL.

## UI restructure

Delete `app/components/approvals/ReviewerMatrixCompany.vue` and `ReviewerMatrixProductLine.vue`. `ReviewerMatrix.vue` becomes one unified table:

- **Top control**: segmented control over `extension_category` (4 buttons). Persist active choice in URL hash (`#cat=skills`), same pattern as today's `#tab=`.
- **Columns**: `Company | Wireless | Datacom | Terminals | Cloud` — productLines sorted by `sortOrder` after the fixed Company column.
- **Rows**: 3-tier indented hierarchy
  - Sticky top row `All (*)` (always visible).
  - 9 macro rows as collapsible group headers (FUNC_CAT_COLORS hint optional). Each expands to its 3 micro children indented.
- **Cells**: chips for admins assigned to **that exact (level, key, tier, plId)**. Inherited coverage shown as a faint inline badge on descendant cells ("+2 from softDev") so the operator sees the effective set without losing the explicit-vs-inherited distinction.
- Per-cell `canEdit` comes from the API annotation; the picker dialog from today's components is lifted to the unified table.

`ReviewerMatrix.nuxt.test.ts` rewritten to cover: category toggle, collapsible macros, inheritance badges, edit-permission gating across all 5 coords.

## Internal API contract

`/api/internal/admin/reviewers/` paths kept as-is for v1 (saves a SPA fetcher rename).

`index.get` response:
```ts
{
  ok: true,
  productLines: [...],
  admins: Array<{
    id, extensionCategory, tier, productLineId,
    categoryLevel, categoryKey,
    userId, userEmail, userName, canEdit
  }>,
  viewer: { isSuperAdmin: boolean, coveringCells: Array<{...5 coords}> }
}
```

`assign.post` + `unassign.delete` payloads gain `extensionCategory, categoryLevel, categoryKey`. `AssignReviewerSchema` in `shared/validators/approvals.ts` gains the matching Zod fields and a refine that checks `categoryKey` membership in the right set (per `categoryLevel`).

## i18n

`taxonomy.l2.<key>` namespace (already present for `SubCatPicker.vue`) extended to all 27 l2 keys in EN + ZH. New keys: `admin.reviewers.matrix.level.{all,macro,micro}`, `admin.reviewers.matrix.cat.{skills,mcp,slash,plugins}`, `admin.reviewers.matrix.inheritedFrom`. Today's `tabs.{company,productLine}` keys are removed.

## Critical files

- `shared/db/schema/approval.ts` — drop & recreate both tables with the shapes above.
- `drizzle/<next>_approval_admins.sql` — generated migration.
- `shared/taxonomy.ts` — add helpers.
- `shared/validators/approvals.ts` + `.test.ts` — extended Zod schemas.
- `shared/approvals/state.ts` — `OfficialTier` unchanged; new `AdminCategoryLevel` type added.
- `server/repositories/reviewers.ts` → rename to `admins.ts`; rewrite all 5 queries (`listMatrix`, `listCellsForUser`, `findAdminsFor`, `findCoveringAdmin`, `findAdminById`).
- `server/utils/auth.ts` — new 5-coord `requireCellAdmin`.
- `server/utils/approvals.ts` — `isReviewerForCell` → `isAdminCovering`; queue list expands via fan-out.
- `server/api/internal/admin/reviewers/{index.get,assign.post,unassign.delete}.ts` — new payloads.
- `server/api/internal/admin/me.ts` — coveringCells annotation.
- `app/pages/admin/reviewers/index.vue` — consume the new shape.
- `app/components/approvals/ReviewerMatrix.vue` — single unified table (replaces today's tabbed shell).
- DELETE: `ReviewerMatrixCompany.vue`, `ReviewerMatrixProductLine.vue`.
- `app/components/approvals/ReviewerMatrix.nuxt.test.ts` — rewrite.
- `tests/integration/repositories/{reviewers,approvals}.test.ts` — rewrite against new schema + fan-out.
- `i18n/locales/{en,zh}.json` — 27 l2 keys + new matrix keys.
- `scripts/seed.ts` — seed `approval_admins` rows for the demo super-admin matching the new shape.

## Edge cases

- **In-flight `approval_requests`**: dropped with the table; pre-launch seed data, no migration burden.
- **Extensions without `l2`**: `snapshot l2=NULL`; routing fan-out simply omits the `micro` candidate — a `micro` admin is unreachable for that request, which is the right semantic.
- **Duplicate notifications when an admin matches at both macro and micro**: SQL is `SELECT DISTINCT user_id` (same dedup as today's `listReviewerQueue` Set), so each user is queued once.

## Verification

```
bun run drizzle:generate
bun run db:reset && SEED_PASSWORD=local bun run db:seed
bun run typecheck && bun run lint
bun run test
bun run test:integration
bun run dev
```

Manual smoke (super-admin sign-in via the seeded `amy@agentcenter.dev`):
1. `/admin/reviewers` — toggle through all 4 extension categories. Each shows a single 5-column table with the 3-tier vertical hierarchy.
2. Assign `(skills, productLine=wireless, macro=softDev)` to user A.
3. Publish an extension as `(category=skills, subCat=softDev, l2=backend)` and request productLine/wireless approval → A's queue picks it up. Inheritance badge appears on the `(skills, wireless, micro=backend)` cell.
4. Sign in as a non-super company admin at `(skills, company, all, *)` → can edit any descendant skills cell, cannot touch `mcp`/`slash`/`plugins` matrices.
5. Revoke (ADR-0001 §Update 2026-06-09) path untouched — confirm an existing revoked extension still renders correctly.

```

81	// Product-line lookup table — bilingual labels, kebab-case ids
82	// (`wireless`, `datacom`, `terminals`, `cloud`). Seeded by migration 0009.
83	// Used as the third axis of the reviewer matrix for the productLine tier,
84	// and as the company-level "endorsing line" on extensions whose
85	// officialTier='productLine'. Editable as data, not code, so adding a line
86	// is a content migration rather than a release.
87	export const productLines = pgTable("product_lines", {
88	  id: text().primaryKey(),
89	  labelEn: text().notNull(),
90	  labelZh: text().notNull(),

95	export const extensions = pgTable(
96	  "extensions",
97	  {
98	    id: text().primaryKey(),
99	    slug: text().notNull().unique(),
100	    category: extensionCategoryEnum().notNull(),
101	    badge: extensionBadgeEnum(),
102	    scope: extensionScopeEnum().notNull(),
103	    // Official-tier approval outcome. Null = unofficial. See approval.ts.
104	    officialTier: officialTierEnum(),
105	    // Endorsing product line — required iff officialTier='productLine',
106	    // null otherwise. CHECK constraint below enforces the shape; the
107	    // approval orchestrator is the only writer. ON DELETE RESTRICT matches
108	    // the iff-rule: SET NULL would still fail the CHECK for every
109	    // productLine-tier row, so RESTRICT just surfaces the real outcome.
110	    productLineId: text().references(() => productLines.id, {
111	      onDelete: "restrict",
112	    }),
113	    // funcCat/subCat are nullable — the redesigned publish wizard does not
114	    // collect them; admin curation can backfill or system defaults apply.
115	    funcCat: funcCatEnum(),
116	    subCat: text(),
117	    l2: text(),
118	    publisherUserId: text().references(() => users.id, {
119	      onDelete: "set null",
120	    }),
121	    // Tier revocation audit trail. Populated when a super-admin demotes
122	    // an Official extension back to Unofficial via the detail page
123	    // Revoke action. Cleared on the next approval — see the orchestrator
124	    // in server/utils/approvals.ts. ON DELETE SET NULL on the admin FK
125	    // so deleting an admin orphans the attribution rather than failing
126	    // the cascade.
127	    revokedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }),
128	    revokedByUserId: text().references(() => users.id, {
129	      onDelete: "set null",
130	    }),
131	    revocationNote: text(),
132	    ownerOrgId: text()
133	      .notNull()
134	      .references(() => organizations.id, { onDelete: "cascade" }),
135	    deptId: text().references(() => departments.id, { onDelete: "set null" }),
136	    iconEmoji: text(),
137	    iconColor: text(),
138	    visibility: visibilityEnum().notNull().default("draft"),
139	    // Editorial flag — hand-curated. Surfaces a single extension on the home
140	    // hero. Coexists with `badge`: badge is a static editorial mark on the
141	    // card; `featured` is a transient "pick of the week" pointer.
142	    featured: boolean().notNull().default(false),
143	    // i18n columns
144	    name: text().notNull(),
145	    nameZh: text(),
146	    tagline: text(),
147	    taglineZh: text(),
148	    description: text(),
149	    descriptionZh: text(),
150	    // Long-form markdown body for the detail page. Not duplicated to ZH yet —

153	    readmeMd: text(),
154	    // metadata for detail page sidebar
155	    homepageUrl: text(),
156	    repoUrl: text(),
157	    licenseSpdx: text(),
158	    compatibilityJson: jsonb(),
159	    // Permissions captured during publish (network/files/runtime/data, etc.).
160	    // Surfaced on the detail page so users see what the extension wants.
161	    permissions: jsonb().notNull().default({}),
162	    // denormalized counters (updated by jobs)
163	    downloadsCount: integer().notNull().default(0),
164	    starsAvg: numeric({ precision: 2, scale: 1 }).notNull().default("0.0"),
165	    ratingsCount: integer().notNull().default(0),
166	    publishedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }),
167	    createdAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),
168	    updatedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),
169	    searchVector: tsvector("search_vector"),
170	  },
171	  (t) => [
172	    index("idx_ext_category").on(t.category),
173	    index("idx_ext_scope").on(t.scope),
174	    index("idx_ext_official_tier").on(t.officialTier),
175	    index("idx_ext_product_line").on(t.productLineId),
176	    index("idx_ext_func_sub_l2").on(t.funcCat, t.subCat, t.l2),
177	    index("idx_ext_dept_path").using(

184	    index("idx_ext_featured_published").on(

1	# ADR-0001: Official-tier approval workflow
2	
3	## Status
4	
5	Accepted — 2026-06-07 (shipped via PR #41 merged as `a392c16`).
6	
7	## Context
8	
9	The product needed a moderation path for elevating a published extension
10	to "Official" status. The shape of the workflow was open along four
11	axes, each with plausible alternatives:
12	
13	1. **What does "category" mean for routing?** The taxonomy already
14	   distinguished `funcCat` (3 top-level enum), `subCat` (text drawn
15	   from `FUNC_TAXONOMY.l1`, 9 leaves), and `deptId` (dotted-path text
16	   PK with descendant matching). Any one could plausibly drive the
17	   reviewer matrix.
18	2. **Are the two official tiers (Product-Line and Company) ordered?**
19	   A hierarchical ladder (PL first, then Company) reads naturally as
20	   "earn trust then escalate." Independent tiers let a publisher
21	   apply directly for Company without a PL gate.
22	3. **How does the existing `extensions.badge` column (already
23	   carrying `"official" | "popular" | "new"` and surfaced through the
24	   frozen `/api/v1` contract) interact with the new tier?** Reusing
25	   `badge` keeps the schema small; introducing a new column keeps the
26	   semantics clean.
27	4. **Who can edit the (tier × category) reviewer matrix?** Any
28	   `role='admin'` membership is the lightest model; a separate
29	   super-admin role is a smaller trust circle.
30	
31	The CLI's `/api/v1` shape is frozen (see `docs/plan.md` §13 and
32	`docs/api.md`), so any choice that changed the public response was a
33	contract break and a much larger lift than the workflow needed.
34	
35	## Decision
36	
37	Four locked decisions, taken together:
38	
39	1. **Category = `subCat`** (the FUNC_TAXONOMY l1 leaf). The matrix is
40	   `9 subCats × 2 tiers = 18` cells — concrete enough to assign
41	   without sprawl, fine-grained enough that a network specialist
42	   isn't asked to review documentation tooling.
43	
44	2. **Tiers are independent, not hierarchical.** A publisher picks
45	   their target tier at submission time; Company supersedes
46	   Product-Line if both end up granted on the same extension. The
47	   orchestrator does not require a prior Product-Line approval before
48	   a Company request.
49	
50	3. **`officialTier` is its own enum column.** `extensions.badge`
51	   stays in the DB unchanged. The `/api/v1/extensions/*` mappers
52	   *derive* `badge: "official"` from `officialTier != null` at the
53	   response boundary, so the frozen contract is preserved without a
54	   v2 bump and the CLI sees no change. App code stops writing `badge`
55	   entirely; future removal can ride an intentional v2 break.
56	
57	4. **Matrix admin = `superAdmin` membership role only.** A new value
58	   is appended to `membershipRoleEnum` alongside `viewer | publisher |
59	   admin`. Reviewers decide their own assigned cells; matrix edits
60	   are a separate, smaller trust circle. Super-admins also bypass the
61	   cell-assignment check when deciding (so they can unblock a stuck
62	   request without first assigning themselves).
63	
64	## Consequences
65	
66	What this makes easier:
67	
68	- The reviewer queue ranks naturally by cell and is small enough that
69	  super-admins can hold the whole matrix in their head.
70	- The CLI keeps working without any contract change — useful since
71	  the CLI is distributed via npm and slow to roll forward.
72	- The state machine is small enough to fit in
73	  `shared/approvals/state.ts` as pure functions with full unit
74	  coverage; the orchestrator (`server/utils/approvals.ts`) does
75	  nothing tricky beyond optimistic-lock race detection.
76	
77	What we gave up:
78	
79	- A single "officially endorsed" enum on `extensions` would have been
80	  one fewer column. The separate `officialTier` means we now have
81	  two fields tracking related concepts; the derive-in-mapper
82	  bridges them but is technically duplication.
83	- The independent-tier model lets a brand-new extension apply for
84	  Company on day one. If we later want to require Product-Line
85	  approval as a prerequisite, that's a state-machine change we'd
86	  need a new ADR for.
87	- A `superAdmin` role concentrates matrix-edit authority in one
88	  account by default. Production deployments need to remember to
89	  seed at least one super-admin (`SEED_SUPER_ADMIN_EMAIL`); without
90	  one, the matrix is unmanageable.
91	
92	Open follow-ups (not blocking):
93	
94	- **Notification fan-out** — the orchestrator emits
95	  `extension/approval.{requested,decided}` Inngest events, but the
96	  current consumer only logs them. Email / in-app notifications are
97	  P1.
98	- **Audit trail for matrix edits** — today we record the decision +
99	  note on each request row but don't capture super-admin matrix
100	  changes. If audit becomes a requirement, add a dedicated
101	  `audit_events` table in a follow-up ADR.
102	
103	## Cross-references
104	
105	- Schema: `shared/db/schema/approval.ts`, `shared/db/schema/extension.ts`
106	- Pure decisions: `shared/approvals/state.ts`
107	- Orchestrator: `server/utils/approvals.ts`
108	- Public-contract bridge: `server/api/v1/extensions/index.get.ts:36`
109	  and `server/api/v1/extensions/[slug]/index.get.ts:24`
110	- CLAUDE.md decision #12 (summary entry pointing here)
111	- `docs/plan.md` §3 (schema additions) and `P21` status entry
112	
113	## Update 2026-06-08 — product-line dimension
114	
115	PR #41's matrix routed exclusively on `(tier × subCat)`. Reality at
116	Naive is that the company is organised in **multiple product lines**
117	(Wireless, Datacom, Terminals, Cloud), and each functional category
118	inside each product line wants its own admin. The original matrix
119	collapses all four lines onto one cell per subCat, which is the wrong
120	unit of trust — a Wireless network specialist isn't the right reviewer
121	for a Cloud network skill. The Product-Line tier therefore needs a
122	third axis. The Company tier stays one admin per subCat — that level
123	is intentionally cross-line.
124	
125	A second constraint surfaced at the same time: the company-tier admin
126	of a given subCat is the right person to **pick the product-line
127	admins** for that same subCat. The original `superAdmin`-only gate
128	makes them ask a super-admin every time, which doesn't scale and
129	doesn't reflect the org chart.
130	
131	Refinements (do not revoke the original four locked decisions; they
132	remain accurate):
133	
134	- **Decision #1 refines**: routing key is `(tier × subCat × productLine?)`,
135	  where `productLine` is required iff `tier='productLine'` and absent
136	  otherwise. The reviewer matrix table widens to
137	  `9 subCats × 1 Company + 9 subCats × 4 ProductLines = 45` cells.
138	  `productLines` is a seeded lookup table (id, label_en, label_zh,
139	  sort_order), kept as data rather than a code enum so adding a line
140	  is a content migration. CHECK constraints on
141	  `approval_reviewers`, `approval_requests`, and `extensions` enforce
142	  the iff-rule at the boundary — repository and orchestrator code can
143	  rely on it as a hard backstop.
144	- **Decision #4 widens**: matrix edits remain super-admin-able anywhere,
145	  but a **company-tier admin of subCat X may now manage productLine
146	  cells of subCat X (any productLine)**. Company-tier cells stay
147	  super-admin-only. The new `requireCellAdmin(event, cell)` helper
148	  encodes the rule; `unassign.delete.ts` loads the target row first so
149	  the gate authorises against the row's own coordinates rather than
150	  the caller's request body.
151	
152	Implementation notes:
153	
154	- **Partial unique indexes over `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`** on
155	  `approval_reviewers`: two indexes, one per tier, each carrying
156	  exactly the columns that participate. This is clearer than one
157	  unique index with quirky NULL semantics and lets each tier's arity
158	  stand on its own in `\d`.
159	- **`/api/v1` contract is unchanged.** `badge: ext.officialTier
160	  ? "official" : ext.badge` already collapses tier away; productLineId
161	  is intentionally suppressed at the same boundary. A future v2 break
162	  could expose the tier + line; until then the CLI sees nothing new.
163	- **`listReviewerQueue` dedup key** picks `productLineId ?? '∅'` for
164	  the super-admin fan-out so a `(company, cloud, null)` cell can't
165	  collide with `(productLine, cloud, '')` in the Set.
166	
167	What this gives up:
168	
169	- Single product line per extension. Multi-line endorsement would
170	  require a `extension_official_product_lines` join table and is
171	  rejected for v1 as additional surface area for a use case nobody has
172	  asked for. A publisher who wants two endorsements submits two
173	  separate requests; the latest approved one stamps the extension.
174	- A non-super company admin can still grow the productLine reviewer
175	  pool unbounded inside their subCat. We accept this — the trust
176	  circle for "your own subCat" is small by construction.
177	
178	Cross-references for this update:
179	
180	- Migration: `drizzle/0009_last_naoko.sql`
181	- New helpers: `server/utils/auth.ts` (`requireCellAdmin`),
182	  `server/repositories/reviewers.ts` (`isCompanyAdminForSubCat`,
183	  `listCompanySubCatsForUser`, `findReviewerById`).
184	- New repo: `server/repositories/productLines.ts`
185	- New endpoint: `server/api/internal/product-lines/index.get.ts`
186	- UI: `app/components/approvals/ReviewerMatrix{,Company,ProductLine}.vue`,
187	  `app/components/approvals/RequestOfficialDialog.vue`,
188	  `app/components/filters/OfficialTierPicker.vue` (collapses the original
189	  OfficialTierPill + ProductLinePill into one popover trigger so the
190	  listing rail stays a single row — see the 2026-06-08-b followup), and the
191	  `extensions.officialTier.productLineWith` i18n key consumed by
192	  `app/components/extension/ExtHero.vue`.
193	
194	## Followup 2026-06-08-b — filter rail consolidation
195	
196	The product-line dimension surfaced as a second inline pill rail
197	(`ProductLinePill.vue`) immediately next to the tier pill rail
198	(`OfficialTierPill.vue`). With `tier=productLine` selected the listing
199	rail rendered roughly twenty interactive elements on one row and wrapped
200	into 2–3 physical lines on a typical viewport — at odds with locked
201	decision #3's "single-row quiet pill rail".
202	
203	The two pill components are replaced by a single popover trigger,
204	`OfficialTierPicker.vue`, that owns both the `tier` and `productLineId`
205	URL keys. The trigger reads `Official: <value>` and matches the
206	existing `CreatorPicker` / `PublisherPicker` / `DeptPicker` affordance.
207	Opening it exposes a Tier row of four options and, when Product-Line is
208	active, a Product-Line row of N+1 options inside the same popover.
209	Switching tier *away* from Product-Line clears `productLineId` in the
210	same `update({...})` call so the URL never carries a stale line on a
211	non-productLine tier — mirroring the iff-rule the server already
212	enforces.
213	
214	No URL contract change. No API change. The active-filter chip strip
215	(`ResultsSummary.vue`) keeps surfacing dismissible chips for the
216	applied filters, unchanged. Cross-references for the followup:
217	
218	- New component: `app/components/filters/OfficialTierPicker.vue`
219	- Filter rail mount: `app/components/filters/FilterBar.vue`
220	- Removed: `app/components/filters/OfficialTierPill.vue`,
221	  `app/components/filters/ProductLinePill.vue`
222	- New i18n key: `filters.tierPicker.triggerLabel` (EN: "Official", ZH: "官方"),
223	  reusing the existing `filters.tierLabel` / `filters.productLineLabel`
224	  keys as popover section headers.
225	
226	## Update 2026-06-09 — tier revocation
227	
228	The original ADR called out tier revocation as the most load-bearing
229	open follow-up: "a super-admin removing a granted tier is not yet
230	supported. Data model allows `null` already; needs a single
231	orchestrator + endpoint." We've now closed it.
232	
233	Design:
234	
235	- **Trigger lives on the detail page**, next to the badge, gated on
236	  `admin/me.isSuperAdmin && officialTier`. Super-admins find an
237	  extension the same way as anyone else — by browsing — and act
238	  spatially next to the thing they're operating on. We deliberately
239	  did not add a dedicated `/admin/officials` list page for v1; if bulk
240	  audits become a need, that's an additive page later.
241	- **Reason note is required**, matching the existing Reject flow.
242	  `RevokeTierSchema` enforces `.trim().min(1)`; the publisher sees the
243	  reason verbatim on their dashboard so a missing note would leave
244	  them in the dark.
245	- **Pending elevation requests are left alone.** Revocation clears the
246	  *current* tier; a pending request asks for a *future* tier. Coupling
247	  them would mean a Company-Official extension with a pending
248	  Wireless productLine request can't be revoked without also
249	  withdrawing the unrelated request — confusing for everyone.
250	
251	Audit trail strategy:
252	
253	- **Three new columns on `extensions`**: `revoked_at`,
254	  `revoked_by_user_id` (FK to `users` with `ON DELETE SET NULL`),
255	  `revocation_note`. Kept on the row itself rather than in a separate
256	  `audit_events` table because the publisher dashboard's "why am I no
257	  longer Official?" answer is one JOIN-free read.
258	- **`setExtensionOfficialTier` extends to clear the trio** in the same
259	  UPDATE whenever a tier is stamped. The invariant "you're either
260	  currently Official with no pending revocation explanation, or
261	  Unofficial" lives in the repo, not scattered across orchestrator
262	  branches.
263	- **`applyRevocation` is a CAS** via `WHERE official_tier IS NOT NULL`:
264	  the returned affected-row count tells the orchestrator whether the
265	  row actually transitioned, so a concurrent revoke / re-approve
266	  surfaces cleanly as `extension_not_official` rather than silently
267	  no-op'ing.
268	
269	Inngest event `extension/tier.revoked` fires with `{ extensionId,
270	revokedByUserId, revokedAt, note }`. The existing notify-approval
271	consumer hasn't been wired to it yet — that's the next follow-up.
272	
273	What we gave up:
274	
275	- A dedicated `audit_events` table would survive across the
276	  Official→Unofficial→Official cycle. Today the columns clear on
277	  re-approval, so once an extension is back to Official the prior
278	  revocation history is gone. That's intentional: the columns answer
279	  "what's happening now?", not "what's ever happened?" If you need
280	  durable history, the table is a separate ADR.
281	
282	Cross-references for this update:
283	
284	- Migration: `drizzle/0011_warm_mandrill.sql`
285	- Schema: `shared/db/schema/extension.ts` (revokedAt / revokedByUserId / revocationNote columns)
286	- Repo: `server/repositories/approvals.ts` (`applyRevocation`,
287	  extended `setExtensionOfficialTier`)
288	- Orchestrator: `server/utils/approvals.ts` (`revokeTier`,
289	  `extension_not_official` code)
290	- Endpoint: `server/api/internal/approvals/revoke.post.ts`
291	- UI: `app/components/extension/ExtHero.vue` (canRevoke prop +
292	  button), `app/components/approvals/RevokeTierDialog.vue`,
293	  `app/pages/extensions/[slug].vue` (admin/me fetch + dialog host),
294	  `app/pages/publish/index.vue` (revocation annotation row)
295	
```
