```
1	// Pure decision functions for the official-tier approval workflow. Mirrors
2	// `shared/extensions/state.ts`: the orchestrator
3	// (`server/utils/approvals.ts`) calls these to figure out *what* to write;
4	// the orchestrator owns the *how* (loading rows, opening transactions,
5	// calling repositories). Keeping the decisions here means they're testable
6	// with no DB and no mocks — just inputs → outputs.
7	
8	export type OfficialTier = "productLine" | "company"
9	
10	export type ApprovalStatus = "pending" | "approved" | "rejected" | "withdrawn"
11	
12	export type ApprovalAction =
13	  | { decision: "approve" }
14	  | { decision: "reject"; note?: string }
15	
16	export interface ApprovalRequestPatch {
17	  status: "approved" | "rejected"
18	  decidedByUserId: string
19	  decidedAt: Date
20	  reviewerNote: string | null
21	}
22	
23	export interface ExtensionOfficialTierPatch {
24	  officialTier: OfficialTier
25	}
26	
27	export interface ApprovalOutcome {
28	  request: ApprovalRequestPatch
29	  // Present iff the request was approved — the orchestrator stamps the new
30	  // tier onto the parent extension in the same transaction.
31	  extension?: ExtensionOfficialTierPatch
32	}
33	
34	// Decide the row updates from a reviewer's verdict. Only pending requests
35	// can transition; other states are no-ops and throw at the orchestrator.
36	export function decideApprovalOutcome(input: {
37	  current: { status: ApprovalStatus; requestedTier: OfficialTier }
38	  action: ApprovalAction
39	  reviewerUserId: string
40	  now: Date
41	}): ApprovalOutcome {
42	  const { current, action, reviewerUserId, now } = input
43	
44	  if (current.status !== "pending") {
45	    throw new Error(
46	      `cannot decide approval request in status '${current.status}'`,
47	    )
48	  }
49	
50	  if (action.decision === "approve") {
51	    return {
52	      request: {
53	        status: "approved",
54	        decidedByUserId: reviewerUserId,
55	        decidedAt: now,
56	        reviewerNote: null,
57	      },
58	      extension: { officialTier: current.requestedTier },
59	    }
60	  }
61	
62	  // Reject — note is preserved verbatim; an empty/whitespace-only note
63	  // collapses to null so downstream "if reviewerNote" reads cleanly.
64	  const trimmed = action.note?.trim()
65	  return {
66	    request: {
67	      status: "rejected",
68	      decidedByUserId: reviewerUserId,
69	      decidedAt: now,
70	      reviewerNote: trimmed && trimmed.length > 0 ? trimmed : null,
71	    },
72	  }
73	}
74	
75	export interface WithdrawOutcome {
76	  request: { status: "withdrawn"; decidedAt: Date }
77	}
78	
79	// A publisher can withdraw a request while it's still pending. Withdrawal
80	// is recorded with a timestamp but no reviewer, since no admin acted.
81	export function decideWithdrawOutcome(input: {
82	  current: { status: ApprovalStatus }
83	  now: Date
84	}): WithdrawOutcome {
85	  if (input.current.status !== "pending") {
86	    throw new Error(
87	      `cannot withdraw approval request in status '${input.current.status}'`,
88	    )
89	  }
90	  return { request: { status: "withdrawn", decidedAt: input.now } }
91	}
92	
```

```
import { existsSync, readFileSync } from "node:fs"
import { resolve } from "node:path"

import { defineConfig } from "drizzle-kit"

const envPath = resolve(".env")
if (existsSync(envPath)) {
  const content = readFileSync(envPath, "utf-8")
  for (const line of content.split("\n")) {
    const match = line.match(/^([A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*)=(.*)$/)
    if (match && !process.env[match[1]]) {
      process.env[match[1]] = match[2].replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "")
    }
  }
}

const databaseUrl = process.env.DATABASE_URL
if (!databaseUrl) {
  throw new Error(
    "Missing DATABASE_URL env var required for Drizzle Kit. " +
      "Set it in .env or your shell before running db:* scripts.",
  )
}

export default defineConfig({
  schema: "./shared/db/schema/index.ts",
  out: "./drizzle",
  dialect: "postgresql",
  dbCredentials: { url: databaseUrl },
  casing: "snake_case",
  verbose: true,
  strict: true,
})
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/db/schema/approval.ts",
  "content": "import { sql } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport {\n  check,\n  index,\n  pgEnum,\n  pgTable,\n  text,\n  timestamp,\n  uniqueIndex,\n} from \"drizzle-orm/pg-core\";\n\nimport { users } from \"./auth\";\nimport {\n  extensionCategoryEnum,\n  extensions,\n  officialTierEnum,\n  productLines,\n} from \"./extension\";\n\nexport const approvalStatusEnum = pgEnum(\"approval_status\", [\n  \"pending\",\n  \"approved\",\n  \"rejected\",\n  \"withdrawn\",\n]);\n\n// Vertical-axis level of a matrix admin assignment.\n// `all` is the wildcard root (categoryKey='*'); `macro` is a FUNC_TAXONOMY\n// l1 key (9 entries); `micro` is an l2 key (27 entries). See\n// `shared/taxonomy.ts` for the keyspaces and the ancestor walk used by\n// the cell-admin gate and the routing fan-out.\nexport const adminCategoryLevelEnum = pgEnum(\"admin_category_level\", [\n  \"all\",\n  \"macro\",\n  \"micro\",\n]);\n\nexport const approvalRequests = pgTable(\n  \"approval_requests\",\n  {\n    id: text().primaryKey(),\n    extensionId: text()\n      .notNull()\n      .references(() => extensions.id, { onDelete: \"cascade\" }),\n    // Snapshot of the extension's category at submission time. Drives the\n    // per-type matrix toggle and is part of the cell key, so we freeze it\n    // here rather than joining `extensions` on every queue scan.\n    extensionCategory: extensionCategoryEnum().notNull(),\n    requestedTier: officialTierEnum().notNull(),\n    // Snapshot of the functional subCat (FUNC_TAXONOMY l1 key) at\n    // submission. Validator layer enforces membership; storing as text\n    // means a taxonomy edit doesn't require a migration.\n    subCat: text().notNull(),\n    // Snapshot of the l2 key (FUNC_TAXONOMY l2). Nullable because not\n    // every extension classifies down to l2; routing fan-out simply\n    // omits the `micro` candidate when l2 is null.\n    l2: text(),\n    // Product line snapshot — required iff requestedTier='productLine'.\n    // RESTRICT on delete keeps the audit trail honest: a product line\n    // cannot disappear while requests still reference it.\n    productLineId: text().references(() => productLines.id, {\n      onDelete: \"restrict\",\n    }),\n    requestedByUserId: text()\n      .notNull()\n      .references(() => users.id, { onDelete: \"cascade\" }),\n    reason: text(),\n    status: approvalStatusEnum().notNull().default(\"pending\"),\n    decidedByUserId: text().references(() => users.id, {\n      onDelete: \"set null\",\n    }),\n    decidedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }),\n    reviewerNote: text(),\n    createdAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),\n    updatedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),\n  },\n  (t) => [\n    // Reviewer queue: pending rows in a given cell, ordered by createdAt.\n    // Extension category leads since the matrix toggles on it; l2 trails\n    // so company-tier rows still collapse onto the same prefix.\n    index(\"idx_approval_status_cell\").on(\n      t.status,\n      t.extensionCategory,\n      t.subCat,\n      t.requestedTier,\n      t.productLineId,\n      t.l2,\n    ),\n    // Publisher \"my requests\" view and the at-most-one-pending guard.\n    index(\"idx_approval_ext_status\").on(t.extensionId, t.status),\n    // Shape invariant: productLineId is present iff requestedTier='productLine'.\n    check(\n      \"approval_requests_pl_shape_chk\",\n      sql`(requested_tier = 'productLine' AND product_line_id IS NOT NULL)\n        OR (requested_tier = 'company' AND product_line_id IS NULL)`,\n    ),\n  ],\n);\n\n// Matrix admin cells. A row in this table grants both (a) admin authority\n// over its covered shadow (descendant cells on either the tier-column or\n// category-level axis) and (b) the duty to decide approval requests routed\n// into that shadow. The cell key is five-dimensional:\n//   (extensionCategory, tier, productLineId?, categoryLevel, categoryKey).\n// Shape invariants (mirroring the iff-pattern used elsewhere):\n//   - productLineId is present iff tier='productLine'.\n//   - categoryKey = '*' iff categoryLevel='all'.\n// Routing fan-out is computed in code by expanding a request's cell into\n// its covering set (≤2 column shapes × ≤3 category shapes) and OR-ing\n// the candidates — see `findAdminsFor` in `server/repositories/admins.ts`.\nexport const approvalAdmins = pgTable(\n  \"approval_admins\",\n  {\n    id: text().primaryKey(),\n    extensionCategory: extensionCategoryEnum().notNull(),\n    tier: officialTierEnum().notNull(),\n    productLineId: text().references(() => productLines.id, {\n      onDelete: \"restrict\",\n    }),\n    categoryLevel: adminCategoryLevelEnum().notNull(),\n    // '*' when categoryLevel='all', otherwise an l1 (macro) or l2 (micro)\n    // key from FUNC_TAXONOMY. Membership in the right keyspace is enforced\n    // at the validator layer — same as approval_requests.subCat — so\n    // adding a taxonomy leaf stays a code-only change.\n    categoryKey: text().notNull(),\n    userId: text()\n      .notNull()\n      .references(() => users.id, { onDelete: \"cascade\" }),\n    createdAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),\n  },\n  (t) => [\n    // Tier-aware uniqueness. ProductLine cells include productLineId in\n    // the key; company cells do not. Two partial indexes are clearer than\n    // relying on NULLS NOT DISTINCT and let each tier's arity stand on\n    // its own in `\\d`.\n    uniqueIndex(\"approval_admins_pl_uq\")\n      .on(\n        t.extensionCategory,\n        t.tier,\n        t.productLineId,\n        t.categoryLevel,\n        t.categoryKey,\n        t.userId,\n      )\n      .where(sql`tier = 'productLine'`),\n    uniqueIndex(\"approval_admins_co_uq\")\n      .on(\n        t.extensionCategory,\n        t.tier,\n        t.categoryLevel,\n        t.categoryKey,\n        t.userId,\n      )\n      .where(sql`tier = 'company'`),\n    // Routing fan-out scan: queue lookups OR over (≤6) candidate cells\n    // with the extensionCategory + tier-column prefix.\n    index(\"idx_admins_cell\").on(\n      t.extensionCategory,\n      t.tier,\n      t.productLineId,\n      t.categoryLevel,\n      t.categoryKey,\n    ),\n    // Covering-row probe for `requireCellAdmin`: starts at (userId) and\n    // narrows to the ≤6 candidate cells.\n    index(\"idx_admins_user\").on(t.userId),\n    // Column-tier shape (same as approval_requests).\n    check(\n      \"approval_admins_pl_shape_chk\",\n      sql`(tier = 'productLine' AND product_line_id IS NOT NULL)\n        OR (tier = 'company' AND product_line_id IS NULL)`,\n    ),\n    // Category-level shape: '*' iff level='all'.\n    check(\n      \"approval_admins_level_shape_chk\",\n      sql`(category_level = 'all' AND category_key = '*')\n        OR (category_level <> 'all' AND category_key <> '*')`,\n    ),\n  ],\n);\n"
}

535:## 11. Validate pipeline
537:`bun run validate` is the primary command — the fast inner loop that mirrors the first four steps of CI. It chains four steps in order, failing fast. The fifth gate, `bun run test:integration`, runs PGlite-backed repository tests; CI runs it on every PR but it's not part of `validate` (kept separate so the local typecheck/test loop stays under a few seconds). Phase 0 landed a working `bun run validate` against the bare scaffold; every subsequent phase keeps it green.
547:### Tools
556:| E2E | Playwright | Same library as the original. Runs against a dev server with seeded data. Local on demand + nightly CI; **not** per-PR. |
558:### What gets tested where
566:- **E2E** (`tests/e2e/*.spec.ts`) — Playwright against a dev server with seeded data. Owns full-stack flows. Slow, not per-PR.
570:### CI
572:`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `bun run validate` plus the integration suite on every PR push:
583:`bun run validate` itself stays fast (no PGlite) — the integration suite is a separate script. CI runs both per-PR; local devs run `test:integration` when touching repositories or schema.
587:### Locked tooling decisions
593:- **`test:integration` is not part of `validate`.** It's a separate, slower gate (still per-PR in CI). Local devs run it explicitly when touching `server/repositories/**` or `shared/db/schema/**`. Same rationale as Playwright before it: keep the local validate loop tight.
594:- **Playwright is not part of `validate`.** It's a separate, slower gate. Run locally with `bun run test:e2e` on demand; CI runs nightly.

748	### Per-phase coverage discipline
749	
750	Each phase PR ships:
751	
752	1. The feature.
753	2. Tests for any new `shared/**` code at the ≥ 95% line threshold.
754	3. Tests for any new endpoint adapter logic via extracted pure functions.
755	4. Component tests for any new interactive component (forms, multi-step flows, anything with branching state).
756	5. A Playwright spec update if the feature is reachable from a new URL.
757	
758	Reviewers reject PRs that ship `shared/**` code without unit tests. This is a hard rule, not a "would be nice." The cost of letting one through is the seed of "we don't really test that area" — easier to never start.
759	
760	### What this does not include
761	
762	Deliberately out of scope for v1:
763	
764	- **Mutation testing** (Stryker): high-value but slow to wire; revisit at v2 if coverage starts feeling like vanity.
765	- **Visual regression** (Playwright screenshots): defer to P13 polish; needs a stable design first.
766	- **Storybook / Histoire** for component documentation: defer; not load-bearing for a v1 codebase.
767	- **External coverage trend service** (Codecov, Coveralls): the local-first deploy target rules out per-PR third-party uploads as a hard dependency. CI artifact is sufficient.
768	
769	---
770	
771	## 13. Commit & PR rules
772	
773	The original Next.js project locked "Conventional Commits, one coherent unit per commit, phase scope." That carries over verbatim. This section spells out the parts that were left informal — granularity, breaking-change handling for `/api/v1`, merge strategy, the per-commit vs. per-PR validate split, and the hook tooling.
774	
775	### Commit message format
776	
777	```
778	<type>(<scope>): <one-line summary, lowercase, no trailing period>
779	
780	<body — optional. Why, not what. Wraps at 72 chars.
781	Reference incidents, surprising decisions, or invariants the diff doesn't show.>
782	
783	<footer — optional. BREAKING CHANGE, Closes #N, …>
784	```
785	
786	### Types
787	
788	`feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`, `perf`, `revert`, `style`. Use the type that describes the dominant change in the commit. A commit that's "feat plus its tests" is `feat`, not split.
789	
790	### Scopes
791	
792	Two scope shapes used here:
793	
794	- **Phase scope** during the v1 build: `p<N>-<short>` matching §10. Example: `feat(p3-browse): wire filter chips to query string`. Use this for any commit that lands a phase deliverable.
795	- **Feature scope** for hotfixes during v1 and all post-v1 work: a short noun for the area touched. Examples: `fix(filter-bar): preserve dept on locale switch`, `refactor(install-button): extract install state machine`, `chore(deps): bump drizzle 0.45 → 0.46`.
796	
797	If a commit spans two scopes evenly, that's a smell — split it.
798	
799	### Commit granularity — one coherent unit per commit
800	
801	A phase is one or several commits. Default to **one coherent unit per commit** — a single focused change that compiles and stands on its own. The exact number of commits per phase depends on the work: a mechanical port may be one commit per phase; a feature build may be several. Optimise for "the diff tells a clear story", not for a target commit count.
802	
803	Calibration:
804	
805	| Granularity | Example | Verdict |
806	|---|---|---|
807	| Too small | `feat: rename variable in ext-card.vue` | No — file-by-file noise |
808	| Right | `chore(p0): install @nuxt/eslint and base config` | Yes |
809	| Right | `feat(p2-db): add extension drizzle schema` | Yes |
810	| Right | `feat(p3-browse): ExtCard component + test` | Yes — code + its tests usually one commit |
811	| Right | `feat(p3-browse): wire ExtCard into home grid` | Yes |
812	| Right | `feat(p15-ui): port shadcn-vue primitives` covering ~28 generated wrappers (Button + Input + Label + Textarea + Checkbox + Skeleton + Dialog × 6 + Sheet × 4 + Popover × 3 + Select × 5 + Tabs × 4) | Yes — single mechanical unit |
813	| Too big | `feat(p3-browse): browse page` covering 8 unrelated files | No — split where the story changes |
814	
815	Tests usually live with the code they cover in the same commit. The exception is adding tests to *existing* code as a coverage-improvement pass — then the test commit stands alone.
816	
817	Always-split boundaries (these go in separate commits even if "small"):
818	
819	- Database schema / migration vs. application code that uses it.
820	- Refactor vs. new feature (refactor first; feature on top).
821	- Generated code (migrations, lockfile updates) vs. source.
822	- Dependency install (`chore`) vs. first use (`feat`).
823	
824	### Per-commit vs. per-PR validate
825	
826	The validate suite has two gates with different strictness:
827	
828	- **Each commit must compile and `bun run typecheck` must pass.** This is the per-step gate. Strict enough to keep history bisectable; loose enough that you can land a Zod validator in one commit before its consumer in the next.
829	- **The PR head must pass full `bun run validate`** (lint + typecheck + test + coverage thresholds). This is the per-PR gate. Enforced by the `pre-push` hook locally and by CI on the PR.
830	
831	This split is intentional. Trying to make every single commit hit 95% coverage on `shared/**` would force unnatural test-first-then-code ordering. Coverage is a PR-boundary property, not a commit-boundary property.
832	
833	### Breaking changes
834	
835	The `/api/v1` contract is the only thing in this repo that can break external consumers (the CLI). If you change a v1 endpoint's response shape, request shape, or status semantics, mark the commit with both `!` and a `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer:
836	
837	```
838	feat(p7-api)!: bundle endpoint returns 410 for archived versions
839	
840	BREAKING CHANGE: GET /api/v1/extensions/:slug/bundle previously returned 503
841	for archived versions; it now returns 410 Gone. CLI < 0.3.0 will misinterpret
842	this. Bump the CLI minimum and document the upgrade in cli/CHANGELOG.md.
843	```
844	
845	CI's contract suite (`tests/contract/`) fails when a v1 response shape changes without these markers — a hard gate, not a convention. Anything else (internal endpoints, components, schema before v1.0 ships) doesn't need the marker; update `docs/plan.md` instead.
846	
847	### Hard rules
848	
849	- **No `--amend` once a commit is on a pushed branch.** Pushed history is shared. Local pre-push amends are fine.
850	- **No `--force` / `--force-with-lease`** on pushed branches. Use a corrective commit (`fix: the previous commit broke X`) or `revert` instead.
851	- **No `--no-verify`.** If a hook is wrong, fix the hook in its own commit; don't bypass.
852	- **No squash-then-amend dance to "clean up" a PR.** PRs are reviewed and merged with their commit history intact. A 20-noisy-commits PR is a PR-shape problem, not a history-cleanup problem — split the work earlier.
853	
854	### Merge strategy
855	
856	PRs merge as **merge commits**, not squash, not rebase. Each phase's commit history is preserved on `main`.
857	
858	Rationale:
859	
860	- The phase-internal commit split exists for a reason — squashing a phase into one commit destroys that.
861	- `git log --first-parent main` gives the high-level view (one entry per merged PR); `git log main` gives the per-commit detail. Both are useful.
862	- `git bisect` is more precise with smaller commits visible on `main`.
863	- Per-step commit messages stay searchable on `main` instead of being collapsed into one PR-shaped blob.
864	
865	Configure GitHub to disable "Allow squash merging" and "Allow rebase merging" on the repo, leaving only "Allow merge commits."
866	
867	### Hooks (Phase 0 deliverable)
```

{

  "content": "import { sql } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport {\n  check,\n  index,\n  pgEnum,\n  pgTable,\n  text,\n  timestamp,\n  uniqueIndex,\n} from \"drizzle-orm/pg-core\";\n\nimport { users } from \"./auth\";\nimport { extensions, officialTierEnum, productLines } from \"./extension\";\n\nexport const approvalStatusEnum = pgEnum(\"approval_status\", [\n  \"pending\",\n  \"approved\",\n  \"rejected\",\n  \"withdrawn\",\n]);\n\nexport const approvalRequests = pgTable(\n  \"approval_requests\",\n  {\n    id: text().primaryKey(),\n    extensionId: text()\n      .notNull()\n      .references(() => extensions.id, { onDelete: \"cascade\" }),\n    requestedTier: officialTierEnum().notNull(),\n    // Snapshot of the functional subCat at submission time. Source of truth is\n    // FUNC_TAXONOMY in shared/taxonomy.ts; validated against the flattened l1\n    // key set at the validator layer.\n    subCat: text().notNull(),\n    // Product line snapshot — required iff requestedTier='productLine'.\n    // RESTRICT on delete keeps the audit trail honest: a product line cannot\n    // disappear while requests still reference it.\n    productLineId: text().references(() => productLines.id, {\n      onDelete: \"restrict\",\n    }),\n    requestedByUserId: text()\n      .notNull()\n      .references(() => users.id, { onDelete: \"cascade\" }),\n    reason: text(),\n    status: approvalStatusEnum().notNull().default(\"pending\"),\n    decidedByUserId: text().references(() => users.id, {\n      onDelete: \"set null\",\n    }),\n    decidedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }),\n    reviewerNote: text(),\n    createdAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),\n    updatedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),\n  },\n  (t) => [\n    // Reviewer queue: pending rows in a given cell, ordered by createdAt.\n    // productLineId trails so company-tier rows (productLineId IS NULL) still\n    // collapse onto the same prefix; productLine-tier scans use the full key.\n    index(\"idx_approval_status_cell\").on(\n      t.status,\n      t.subCat,\n      t.requestedTier,\n      t.productLineId,\n    ),\n    // Publisher \"my requests\" view and the at-most-one-pending guard.\n    index(\"idx_approval_ext_status\").on(t.extensionId, t.status),\n    // Shape invariant: productLineId is present iff requestedTier='productLine'.\n    check(\n      \"approval_requests_pl_shape_chk\",\n      sql`(requested_tier = 'productLine' AND product_line_id IS NOT NULL)\n        OR (requested_tier = 'company' AND product_line_id IS NULL)`,\n    ),\n  ],\n);\n\nexport const approvalReviewers = pgTable(\n  \"approval_reviewers\",\n  {\n    id: text().primaryKey(),\n    tier: officialTierEnum().notNull(),\n    // Same FUNC_TAXONOMY l1 key as approvalRequests.subCat — text rather\n    // than enum so adding a taxonomy leaf doesn't require a migration.\n    subCat: text().notNull(),\n    // Product line this reviewer is scoped to. Required iff tier='productLine',\n    // null when tier='company'. CHECK enforces the shape; the unique behaviour\n    // for each tier is expressed below as two partial indexes.\n    productLineId: text().references(() => productLines.id, {\n      onDelete: \"restrict\",\n    }),\n    userId: text()\n      .notNull()\n      .references(() => users.id, { onDelete: \"cascade\" }),\n    createdAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),\n  },\n  (t) => [\n    // Tier-aware uniqueness. ProductLine cells include productLineId in the\n    // key; company cells do not. Two partial indexes are clearer than relying\n    // on NULLS NOT DISTINCT and let each tier's arity stand on its own.\n    uniqueIndex(\"approval_reviewers_pl_cell_user_uq\")\n      .on(t.tier, t.subCat, t.productLineId, t.userId)\n      .where(sql`tier = 'productLine'`),\n    uniqueIndex(\"approval_reviewers_co_cell_user_uq\")\n      .on(t.tier, t.subCat, t.userId)\n      .where(sql`tier = 'company'`),\n    index(\"idx_approval_reviewers_cell\").on(\n      t.tier,\n      t.subCat,\n      t.productLineId,\n    ),\n    // Shape invariant: productLineId is present iff tier='productLine'.\n    check(\n      \"approval_reviewers_pl_shape_chk\",\n      sql`(tier = 'productLine' AND product_line_id IS NOT NULL)\n        OR (tier = 'company' AND product_line_id IS NULL)`,\n    ),\n  ],\n);\n"
}

1	import { describe, it, expect } from "vitest"
2	import { FUNC_TAXONOMY, FUNC_CAT_COLORS } from "~~/shared/taxonomy"
3	
4	describe("taxonomy", () => {
5	  it("has exactly three top-level func categories", () => {
6	    expect(FUNC_TAXONOMY.map((n) => n.key).sort()).toEqual([
7	      "business",
8	      "tools",
9	      "workTask",
10	    ])
11	  })
12	
13	  it("every node has three l1 entries, each with three l2 entries", () => {
14	    for (const node of FUNC_TAXONOMY) {
15	      expect(node.l1).toHaveLength(3)
16	      for (const l1 of node.l1) {
17	        expect(l1.l2).toHaveLength(3)
18	        expect(typeof l1.key).toBe("string")
19	        for (const l2 of l1.l2) expect(typeof l2).toBe("string")
20	      }
21	    }
22	  })
23	
24	  it("l1 keys are unique across the whole taxonomy", () => {
25	    const all = FUNC_TAXONOMY.flatMap((n) => n.l1.map((l) => l.key))
26	    expect(new Set(all).size).toBe(all.length)
27	  })
28	
29	  it("FUNC_CAT_COLORS has an entry for every FuncCatKey", () => {
30	    for (const node of FUNC_TAXONOMY) {
31	      expect(FUNC_CAT_COLORS[node.key]).toMatch(/^oklch\(/)
32	    }
33	  })
34	})

1	import { z } from "zod"
2	
3	import { FUNC_TAXONOMY } from "~~/shared/taxonomy"
4	
5	// SubCat = the l1 leaf inside FUNC_TAXONOMY (e.g. "systemDesign",
6	// "softDev", "network", "embedded"). Flatten at module load so the
7	// validator runs O(1) without re-traversing the constant on each call.
8	// `SUB_CAT_KEY_LIST` is derived from the Set so the two stay in sync.
9	const SUB_CAT_KEYS = new Set<string>(
10	  FUNC_TAXONOMY.flatMap((c) => c.l1.map((s) => s.key)),
11	)
12	
13	export const SUB_CAT_KEY_LIST: readonly string[] = [...SUB_CAT_KEYS]
14	
15	export const OfficialTier = z.enum(["productLine", "company"])
16	export type OfficialTier = z.infer<typeof OfficialTier>
17	
18	export const SubCatKey = z.string().refine((v) => SUB_CAT_KEYS.has(v), {
19	  message: "must be a key from FUNC_TAXONOMY",
20	})
21	
22	// Product-line ids are seeded text PKs in kebab-case (wireless, datacom,
23	// terminals, cloud as of 0009). Validate the shape here; the DB FK + CHECK
24	// constraint enforces membership and the iff-rule against the actual tier.
25	export const ProductLineId = z
26	  .string()
27	  .trim()
28	  .min(1)
29	  .max(40)
30	  .regex(/^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$/, {
31	    message: "must be a kebab-case product-line id",
32	  })
33	export type ProductLineId = z.infer<typeof ProductLineId>
34	
35	// iff-rule helpers — the DB CHECK is the source of truth, but surfacing
36	// the rule at the validator layer turns a bad request into a typed Zod
37	// error instead of a generic 23514 from Postgres.
38	const requireProductLineIff = <T extends {
39	  tier?: OfficialTier
40	  requestedTier?: OfficialTier
41	  productLineId?: string | undefined
42	}>(
43	  v: T,
44	  tierKey: "tier" | "requestedTier",
45	): boolean => {
46	  const tier = v[tierKey]
47	  const hasPl = !!v.productLineId
48	  if (tier === "productLine") return hasPl
49	  if (tier === "company") return !hasPl
50	  return true
51	}
52	
53	export const APPROVAL_REASON_MAX = 500
54	export const APPROVAL_NOTE_MAX = 500
55	export const REVOKE_NOTE_MAX = 500
56	
57	const optionalText = (max: number) =>
58	  z
59	    .string()
60	    .trim()
61	    .max(max)
62	    .optional()
63	    .transform((v) => (v && v.length > 0 ? v : undefined))
64	
65	export const SubmitApprovalSchema = z
66	  .object({
67	    extensionId: z.string().trim().min(1),
68	    requestedTier: OfficialTier,
69	    subCat: SubCatKey,
70	    productLineId: ProductLineId.optional(),
71	    reason: optionalText(APPROVAL_REASON_MAX),
72	  })
73	  .refine((v) => requireProductLineIff(v, "requestedTier"), {
74	    path: ["productLineId"],
75	    message: "productLineId is required iff requestedTier=productLine",
76	  })
77	export type SubmitApprovalInput = z.infer<typeof SubmitApprovalSchema>
78	
79	// Discriminated union: a reviewer note only makes sense on a rejection.
80	// The orchestrator drops any note sent with an approve action anyway —
81	// making that explicit at the API boundary catches the mistake before
82	// it crosses the wire instead of silently after.
83	export const DecideApprovalSchema = z.discriminatedUnion("decision", [
84	  z.object({
85	    requestId: z.string().trim().min(1),
86	    decision: z.literal("approve"),
87	  }),
88	  z.object({
89	    requestId: z.string().trim().min(1),
90	    decision: z.literal("reject"),
91	    note: optionalText(APPROVAL_NOTE_MAX),
92	  }),
93	])
94	export type DecideApprovalInput = z.infer<typeof DecideApprovalSchema>
95	
96	export const WithdrawApprovalSchema = z.object({
97	  requestId: z.string().trim().min(1),
98	})
99	export type WithdrawApprovalInput = z.infer<typeof WithdrawApprovalSchema>
100	
101	// Super-admin Revoke action from the detail page. The note is REQUIRED
102	// (mirrors the existing reject flow) — the publisher dashboard surfaces
103	// it directly so a missing note would leave the publisher in the dark.
104	// `.trim().min(1)` enforces "non-empty after trim" so whitespace-only
105	// submissions are caught at the validator boundary.
106	export const RevokeTierSchema = z.object({
107	  extensionId: z.string().trim().min(1),
108	  note: z.string().trim().min(1).max(REVOKE_NOTE_MAX),
109	})
110	export type RevokeTierInput = z.infer<typeof RevokeTierSchema>
111	
112	export const AssignReviewerSchema = z
113	  .object({
114	    tier: OfficialTier,
115	    subCat: SubCatKey,
116	    productLineId: ProductLineId.optional(),
117	    userId: z.string().trim().min(1),
118	  })
119	  .refine((v) => requireProductLineIff(v, "tier"), {
120	    path: ["productLineId"],
121	    message: "productLineId is required iff tier=productLine",
122	  })
123	export type AssignReviewerInput = z.infer<typeof AssignReviewerSchema>
124	
125	export const UnassignReviewerSchema = z.object({
126	  id: z.string().trim().min(1),
127	})
128	export type UnassignReviewerInput = z.infer<typeof UnassignReviewerSchema>
129	
130	export const ListApprovalsQuerySchema = z.object({
131	  // "mine"  → requests this user submitted as a publisher
132	  // "queue" → pending requests routed to this user as a reviewer
133	  view: z.enum(["mine", "queue"]).default("mine"),
134	  // Reviewer-queue narrowing keys. Reused from the cell-shape validators
135	  // above so the queue picker stays in lockstep with the publisher dialog
136	  // and the listing rail. No iff-rule between tier and productLineId here:
137	  // a reviewer can legitimately narrow to "any Wireless requests" across
138	  // every subCat without being forced to pick a tier first.
139	  tier: OfficialTier.optional(),
140	  subCat: SubCatKey.optional(),
141	  productLineId: ProductLineId.optional(),
142	})
143	export type ListApprovalsQuery = z.infer<typeof ListApprovalsQuerySchema>
144	
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/taxonomy.ts",
  "content": "import type { FuncCatKey, FuncTaxonomyNode } from \"~~/shared/types\"\n\nexport const FUNC_TAXONOMY: FuncTaxonomyNode[] = [\n  {\n    key: \"workTask\",\n    l1: [\n      { key: \"systemDesign\", l2: [\"reqAnalysis\", \"funcDesign\", \"archDesign\"] },\n      { key: \"softDev\", l2: [\"frontend\", \"backend\", \"devops\"] },\n      { key: \"testing\", l2: [\"unitTest\", \"intTest\", \"perfTest\"] },\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    key: \"business\",\n    l1: [\n      { key: \"network\", l2: [\"http\", \"grpc\", \"mqtt\"] },\n      { key: \"embedded\", l2: [\"rtos\", \"firmware\", \"drivers\"] },\n      { key: \"cloud\", l2: [\"aws\", \"azure\", \"k8s\"] },\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    key: \"tools\",\n    l1: [\n      { key: \"docs\", l2: [\"markdown\", \"pdf\", \"wiki\"] },\n      { key: \"data\", l2: [\"csv\", \"sql\", \"viz\"] },\n      { key: \"vcs\", l2: [\"git\", \"pr\", \"cicd\"] },\n    ],\n  },\n]\n\nexport const FUNC_CAT_COLORS: Record<FuncCatKey, string> = {\n  workTask: \"oklch(62% 0.18 255)\",\n  business: \"oklch(62% 0.18 145)\",\n  tools: \"oklch(62% 0.18 55)\",\n}\n\n// Vertical-axis level for the approval admin matrix. Lines up 1-1 with\n// the `admin_category_level` DB enum on `approval_admins`.\n//   all   → wildcard (`categoryKey='*'`); covers every macro and micro.\n//   macro → an l1 leaf in FUNC_TAXONOMY (e.g. \"systemDesign\"); covers\n//           itself + its three l2 children.\n//   micro → an l2 leaf (e.g. \"reqAnalysis\"); covers only itself.\nexport type CategoryLevel = \"all\" | \"macro\" | \"micro\"\n\n// Built once at module load by walking FUNC_TAXONOMY. Same pattern as\n// `SUB_CAT_KEYS` in shared/validators/approvals.ts — derive from the\n// constant so a taxonomy edit can't drift the keysets out of sync.\nconst L1_TO_L2: ReadonlyMap<string, readonly string[]> = (() => {\n  const map = new Map<string, readonly string[]>()\n  for (const node of FUNC_TAXONOMY) {\n    for (const l1 of node.l1) {\n      map.set(l1.key, Object.freeze([...l1.l2]))\n    }\n  }\n  return map\n})()\n\nconst L2_TO_L1: ReadonlyMap<string, string> = (() => {\n  const map = new Map<string, string>()\n  for (const node of FUNC_TAXONOMY) {\n    for (const l1 of node.l1) {\n      for (const l2 of l1.l2) map.set(l2, l1.key)\n    }\n  }\n  return map\n})()\n\nexport const ALL_L1_KEYS: readonly string[] = Object.freeze([\n  ...L1_TO_L2.keys(),\n])\nexport const ALL_L2_KEYS: readonly string[] = Object.freeze([\n  ...L2_TO_L1.keys(),\n])\n\nexport function l2KeysFor(l1: string): readonly string[] {\n  return L1_TO_L2.get(l1) ?? []\n}\n\nexport function l1KeyFor(l2: string): string | null {\n  return L2_TO_L1.get(l2) ?? null\n}\n\nexport function isL1Key(k: string): boolean {\n  return L1_TO_L2.has(k)\n}\n\nexport function isL2Key(k: string): boolean {\n  return L2_TO_L1.has(k)\n}\n\n// Self + ancestors up to (all, '*'). Drives both `requireCellAdmin`\n// (covering-row probe) and the routing fan-out in\n// `server/repositories/admins.ts:findAdminsFor`.\n//   ('micro', l2)  → [(micro, l2), (macro, l1Parent), (all, '*')]\n//   ('macro', l1)  → [(macro, l1), (all, '*')]\n//   ('all',   '*') → [(all, '*')]\n// Unknown keys collapse to just (all, '*') so an unrecognised input\n// can still be authorised by the wildcard cell rather than throwing —\n// the validator layer is responsible for rejecting bad inputs before\n// they reach here.\nexport function categoryAncestors(\n  level: CategoryLevel,\n  key: string,\n): Array<{ level: CategoryLevel; key: string }> {\n  const out: Array<{ level: CategoryLevel; key: string }> = []\n  if (level === \"micro\") {\n    const parent = l1KeyFor(key)\n    out.push({ level: \"micro\", key })\n    if (parent) out.push({ level: \"macro\", key: parent })\n  } else if (level === \"macro\") {\n    out.push({ level: \"macro\", key })\n  }\n  out.push({ level: \"all\", key: \"*\" })\n  return out\n}\n"
}

expect([...l2KeysFor(\"softDev\")]).toEqual([\"frontend\", \"backend\", \"devops\"])\n  })\n\n  it(\"l2KeysFor returns an empty array for unknown keys\", () => {\n    expect(l2KeysFor(\"not-a-real-key\")).toEqual([])\n  })\n})\n\ndescribe(\"isL1Key / isL2Key\", () => {\n  it(\"recognises known l1 and l2 keys\", () => {\n    expect(isL1Key(\"systemDesign\")).toBe(true)\n    expect(isL2Key(\"reqAnalysis\")).toBe(true)\n  })\n\n  it(\"returns false for cross-level or unknown inputs\", () => {\n expect(isL1Key(\"*\")).toBe(false)\n  })\n})\n\ndescribe(\"categoryAncestors\", () => {\n  it(\"walks micro → macro → all\", () => {\n    expect(categoryAncestors(\"micro\", })\n\n  it(\"'all' is its own only ancestor\", () => {\n    expect(categoryAncestors(\"all\", \"*\")).toEqual([\n      { level: \"all\", key: \"*\" },\n    ])\n  })\n\n  it(\"collapses an unrecognised {
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/db/schema/approval.ts",
  "content": "import { sql } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport {\n  check,\n  index,\n  pgEnum,\n  pgTable,\n  text,\n  timestamp,\n  uniqueIndex,\n} from \"drizzle-orm/pg-core\";\n\nimport { users } from \"./auth\";\nimport {\n  extensionCategoryEnum,\n  extensions,\n  officialTierEnum,\n  productLines,\n} from \"./extension\";\n\nexport const approvalStatusEnum = pgEnum(\"approval_status\", [\n  \"pending\",\n  \"approved\",\n  \"rejected\",\n  \"withdrawn\",\n]);\n\n// Vertical-axis level of a matrix admin assignment.\n// `all` is the wildcard root (categoryKey='*'); `macro` is a FUNC_TAXONOMY\n// l1 key (9 entries); `micro` is an l2 key (27 entries). See\n// `shared/taxonomy.ts` for the keyspaces and the ancestor walk used by\n// the cell-admin gate and the routing fan-out.\nexport const adminCategoryLevelEnum = pgEnum(\"admin_category_level\", [\n  \"all\",\n  \"macro\",\n  \"micro\",\n]);\n\nexport const approvalRequests = pgTable(\n  \"approval_requests\",\n  {\n    id: text().primaryKey(),\n    extensionId: text()\n      .notNull()\n      .references(() => extensions.id, { onDelete: \"cascade\" }),\n    // Snapshot of the extension's category at submission time. Drives the\n    // per-type matrix toggle and is part of the cell key, so we freeze it\n    // here rather than joining `extensions` on every queue scan.\n    extensionCategory: extensionCategoryEnum().notNull(),\n    requestedTier: officialTierEnum().notNull(),\n    // Snapshot of the functional subCat (FUNC_TAXONOMY l1 key) at\n    // submission. Validator layer enforces membership; storing as text\n    // means a taxonomy edit doesn't require a migration.\n    subCat: text().notNull(),\n    // Snapshot of the l2 key (FUNC_TAXONOMY l2). Nullable because not\n    // every extension classifies down to l2; routing fan-out simply\n    // omits the `micro` candidate when l2 is null.\n    l2: text(),\n    // Product line snapshot — required iff requestedTier='productLine'.\n    // RESTRICT on delete keeps the audit trail honest: a product line\n    // cannot disappear while requests still reference it.\n    productLineId: text().references(() => productLines.id, {\n      onDelete: \"restrict\",\n    }),\n    requestedByUserId: text()\n      .notNull()\n      .references(() => users.id, { onDelete: \"cascade\" }),\n    reason: text(),\n    status: approvalStatusEnum().notNull().default(\"pending\"),\n    decidedByUserId: text().references(() => users.id, {\n      onDelete: \"set null\",\n    }),\n    decidedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }),\n    reviewerNote: text(),\n    createdAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),\n    updatedAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),\n  },\n  (t) => [\n    // Reviewer queue: pending rows in a given cell. Extension category\n    // leads since the matrix toggles on it; l2 trails so company-tier\n    // rows still collapse onto the same prefix.\n    index(\"idx_approval_status_cell\").on(\n      t.status,\n      t.extensionCategory,\n      t.subCat,\n      t.requestedTier,\n      t.productLineId,\n      t.l2,\n    ),\n    // Publisher \"my requests\" view and the at-most-one-pending guard.\n    index(\"idx_approval_ext_status\").on(t.extensionId, t.status),\n    // Shape invariant: productLineId is present iff requestedTier='productLine'.\n    check(\n      \"approval_requests_pl_shape_chk\",\n      sql`(requested_tier = 'productLine' AND product_line_id IS NOT NULL)\n        OR (requested_tier = 'company' AND product_line_id IS NULL)`,\n    ),\n  ],\n);\n\n// Matrix admin cells. A row in this table grants both (a) admin authority\n// over its covered shadow (descendant cells on either the tier-column or\n// category-level axis) and (b) the duty to decide approval requests routed\n// into that shadow. Replaces the pre-redesign `approval_reviewers` (which\n// had a flat 3-coord cell key); see ADR-0001's \"Update 2026-06-09b\" entry\n// for the rationale.\n//\n// Cell key (5-dim):\n//   (extensionCategory, tier, productLineId?, categoryLevel, categoryKey).\n//\n// Shape invariants (mirroring the iff-pattern used elsewhere):\n//   - productLineId is present iff tier='productLine'.\n//   - categoryKey = '*' iff categoryLevel='all'.\n//\n// Routing fan-out is computed in code by expanding a request's cell into\n// its covering set (≤2 column shapes × ≤3 category shapes) and OR-ing the\n// candidates — see `findAdminsFor` in `server/repositories/admins.ts`.\nexport const approvalAdmins = pgTable(\n  \"approval_admins\",\n  {\n    id: text().primaryKey(),\n    extensionCategory: extensionCategoryEnum().notNull(),\n    tier: officialTierEnum().notNull(),\n    productLineId: text().references(() => productLines.id, {\n      onDelete: \"restrict\",\n    }),\n    categoryLevel: adminCategoryLevelEnum().notNull(),\n    // '*' when categoryLevel='all', otherwise an l1 (macro) or l2 (micro)\n    // key from FUNC_TAXONOMY. Membership in the right keyspace is enforced\n    // at the validator layer — same as approval_requests.subCat — so\n    // adding a taxonomy leaf stays a code-only change.\n    categoryKey: text().notNull(),\n    userId: text()\n      .notNull()\n      .references(() => users.id, { onDelete: \"cascade\" }),\n    createdAt: timestamp({ withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),\n  },\n  (t) => [\n    // Tier-aware uniqueness. ProductLine cells include productLineId in\n    // the key; company cells do not. Two partial indexes are clearer than\n    // relying on NULLS NOT DISTINCT and let each tier's arity stand on\n    // its own in `\\d`.\n    uniqueIndex(\"approval_admins_pl_uq\")\n      .on(\n        t.extensionCategory,\n        t.tier,\n        t.productLineId,\n        t.categoryLevel,\n        t.categoryKey,\n        t.userId,\n      )\n      .where(sql`tier = 'productLine'`),\n    uniqueIndex(\"approval_admins_co_uq\")\n      .on(\n        t.extensionCategory,\n        t.tier,\n        t.categoryLevel,\n        t.categoryKey,\n        t.userId,\n      )\n      .where(sql`tier = 'company'`),\n    // Routing fan-out scan: queue lookups OR over (≤6) candidate cells\n    // with the extensionCategory + tier-column prefix.\n    index(\"idx_admins_cell\").on(\n      t.extensionCategory,\n      t.tier,\n      t.productLineId,\n      t.categoryLevel,\n      t.categoryKey,\n    ),\n    // Covering-row probe for `requireCellAdmin`: starts at (userId) and\n    // narrows to the ≤6 candidate cells.\n    index(\"idx_admins_user\").on(t.userId),\n    // Column-tier shape (same as approval_requests).\n    check(\n      \"approval_admins_pl_shape_chk\",\n      sql`(tier = 'productLine' AND product_line_id IS NOT NULL)\n        OR (tier = 'company' AND product_line_id IS NULL)`,\n    ),\n    // Category-level shape: '*' iff level='all'.\n    check(\n      \"approval_admins_level_shape_chk\",\n      sql`(category_level = 'all' AND category_key = '*')\n        OR (category_level <> 'all' AND category_key <> '*')`,\n    ),\n  ],\n);\n"
}

1	import * as approvalsRepo from "~~/server/repositories/approvals"
2	import * as extensionsRepo from "~~/server/repositories/extensions"
3	import * as reviewersRepo from "~~/server/repositories/reviewers"
4	import {
5	  decideApprovalOutcome,
6	  decideWithdrawOutcome,
7	  type ApprovalAction,
8	  type OfficialTier,
9	} from "~~/shared/approvals/state"
10	
11	import { useDb } from "./db"
12	import { safeSend } from "./inngest"
13	
14	// Orchestrators over the approvals/reviewers/extensions repositories. Pure
15	// transitions live in `shared/approvals/state.ts`; this file loads rows,
16	// opens transactions, fires Inngest events, and maps domain errors to
17	// HTTP-shaped codes the endpoints translate.
18	
19	export type ApprovalErrorCode =
20	  | "extension_not_found"
21	  | "not_publisher_owner"
22	  | "extension_not_published"
23	  | "request_not_found"
24	  | "request_not_pending"
25	  | "duplicate_pending_request"
26	  | "not_reviewer"
27	  | "not_requester"
28	  | "missing_product_line"
29	  | "unexpected_product_line"
30	  | "extension_not_official"
31	
32	export class ApprovalError extends Error {
33	  readonly code: ApprovalErrorCode
34	  constructor(code: ApprovalErrorCode) {
35	    super(code)
36	    this.code = code
37	    this.name = "ApprovalError"
38	  }
39	}
40	
41	export interface SubmitParams {
42	  extensionId: string
43	  requestedTier: OfficialTier
44	  subCat: string
45	  productLineId: string | null
46	  userId: string
47	  reason: string | undefined
48	}
49	
50	export async function submitRequest(
51	  params: SubmitParams,
52	): Promise<approvalsRepo.ApprovalRequestRow> {
53	  // Shape rule mirrors the DB CHECK: productLineId is present iff the
54	  // requested tier is `productLine`. Surfacing both error codes lets the
55	  // endpoint give a precise message instead of bouncing the raw 23514.
56	  if (params.requestedTier === "productLine" && !params.productLineId) {
57	    throw new ApprovalError("missing_product_line")
58	  }
59	  if (params.requestedTier === "company" && params.productLineId) {
60	    throw new ApprovalError("unexpected_product_line")
61	  }
62	
63	  const db = useDb()
64	
65	  const ext = await extensionsRepo.findById(db, params.extensionId)
66	  if (!ext) throw new ApprovalError("extension_not_found")
67	  if (ext.publisherUserId !== params.userId) {
68	    throw new ApprovalError("not_publisher_owner")
69	  }
70	  if (ext.visibility !== "published") {
71	    throw new ApprovalError("extension_not_published")
72	  }
73	
74	  const row = await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
75	    // Re-check inside the transaction — race window between the visibility
76	    // load above and the insert below would otherwise let a duplicate slip
77	    // through. PG isolation is `read committed` so the second check still
78	    // sees a concurrent insert that already committed.
79	    const existing = await approvalsRepo.findPendingByExtension(
80	      tx,
81	      params.extensionId,
82	    )
83	    if (existing) throw new ApprovalError("duplicate_pending_request")
84	
85	    return approvalsRepo.insertRequest(tx, {
86	      id: crypto.randomUUID(),
87	      extensionId: params.extensionId,
88	      requestedTier: params.requestedTier,
89	      subCat: params.subCat,
90	      productLineId: params.productLineId,
91	      requestedByUserId: params.userId,
92	      reason: params.reason ?? null,
93	    })
94	  })
95	
96	  // Best-effort: the request row is the source of truth and is already
97	  // visible in the reviewer queue. A transient Inngest outage (or, in dev,
98	  // a missing `inngest-cli dev` process) must not fail the user's submit
99	  // after the row has been committed.
100	  await safeSend({
101	    name: "extension/approval.requested",
102	    data: {
103	      requestId: row.id,
104	      extensionId: row.extensionId,
105	      requestedTier: row.requestedTier,
106	      subCat: row.subCat,
107	      productLineId: row.productLineId,
108	      requesterUserId: row.requestedByUserId,
109	    },
110	  })
111	
112	  return row
113	}
114	
115	export interface DecideParams {
116	  requestId: string
117	  action: ApprovalAction
118	  reviewerUserId: string
119	}
120	
121	export async function decideRequest(
122	  params: DecideParams,
123	): Promise<approvalsRepo.ApprovalRequestRow> {
124	  const db = useDb()
125	
126	  const current = await approvalsRepo.findById(db, params.requestId)
127	  if (!current) throw new ApprovalError("request_not_found")
128	  if (current.status !== "pending") {
129	    throw new ApprovalError("request_not_pending")
130	  }
131	
132	  // Super-admins can decide on any cell; otherwise the reviewer must be
133	  // assigned to the exact (requestedTier, subCat, productLineId?) cell this
134	  // request was filed against. Tier mismatches don't leak across cells.
135	  const allowed =
136	    (await reviewersRepo.isSuperAdmin(db, params.reviewerUserId)) ||
137	    (await approvalsRepo.isReviewerForCell(
138	      db,
139	      params.reviewerUserId,
140	      current.requestedTier,
141	      current.subCat,
142	      current.productLineId,
143	    ))
144	  if (!allowed) throw new ApprovalError("not_reviewer")
145	
146	  const outcome = decideApprovalOutcome({
147	    current: {
148	      status: current.status,
149	      requestedTier: current.requestedTier,
150	    },
151	    action: params.action,
152	    reviewerUserId: params.reviewerUserId,
153	    now: new Date(),
154	  })
155	
156	  // Optimistic locking lives inside the repository's UPDATE — if a second
157	  // reviewer decided between the pre-check above and this transaction, the
158	  // affected row count comes back zero and we surface the race as
159	  // `request_not_pending`.
160	  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
161	    const updated = await approvalsRepo.applyDecision(
162	      tx,
163	      params.requestId,
164	      outcome.request,
165	    )
166	    if (updated === 0) throw new ApprovalError("request_not_pending")
167	    if (outcome.extension) {
168	      await approvalsRepo.setExtensionOfficialTier(
169	        tx,
170	        current.extensionId,
171	        outcome.extension.officialTier,
172	        current.productLineId,
173	      )
174	    }
175	  })
176	
177	  const updated = await approvalsRepo.findById(db, params.requestId)
178	  if (!updated) throw new Error("approval request vanished mid-decide")
179	
180	  // Best-effort notification after commit; see submitRequest for rationale.
181	  await safeSend({
182	    name: "extension/approval.decided",
183	    data: {
184	      requestId: updated.id,
185	      extensionId: updated.extensionId,
186	      decision: outcome.request.status,
187	      decidedByUserId: params.reviewerUserId,
188	    },
189	  })
190	
191	  return updated
192	}
193	
194	export interface WithdrawParams {
195	  requestId: string
196	  userId: string
197	}
198	
199	export async function withdrawRequest(
200	  params: WithdrawParams,
201	): Promise<approvalsRepo.ApprovalRequestRow> {
202	  const db = useDb()
203	
204	  const current = await approvalsRepo.findById(db, params.requestId)
205	  if (!current) throw new ApprovalError("request_not_found")
206	  if (current.requestedByUserId !== params.userId) {
207	    throw new ApprovalError("not_requester")
208	  }
209	  if (current.status !== "pending") {
210	    throw new ApprovalError("request_not_pending")
211	  }
212	
213	  const outcome = decideWithdrawOutcome({
214	    current: { status: current.status },
215	    now: new Date(),
216	  })
217	  const affected = await approvalsRepo.applyWithdraw(
218	    db,
219	    params.requestId,
220	    outcome.request.decidedAt,
221	  )
222	  if (affected === 0) throw new ApprovalError("request_not_pending")
223	
224	  const updated = await approvalsRepo.findById(db, params.requestId)
225	  if (!updated) throw new Error("approval request vanished mid-withdraw")
226	  return updated
227	}
228	
229	export interface RevokeTierParams {
230	  extensionId: string
231	  superAdminUserId: string
232	  note: string
233	}
234	
235	export interface RevokeTierResult {
236	  extensionId: string
237	  revokedAt: Date
238	}
239	
240	// Super-admin Revoke action from the detail page. Loads the extension,
241	// pre-checks that it's actually Official, then atomically clears tier +
242	// productLineId and writes the audit trio. The DB-level WHERE clause in
243	// applyRevocation re-checks officialTier IS NOT NULL so a concurrent
244	// revoke / re-approval loses the race and surfaces as
245	// `extension_not_official` rather than silently clobbering the row.
246	// Caller is expected to already be a super-admin (gated at the endpoint
247	// via requireSuperAdmin) — we don't re-check here for the same reason the
248	// reviewer-assign endpoint doesn't re-check its gate.
249	export async function revokeTier(
250	  params: RevokeTierParams,
251	): Promise<RevokeTierResult> {
252	  const db = useDb()
253	  const ext = await extensionsRepo.findById(db, params.extensionId)
254	  if (!ext) throw new ApprovalError("extension_not_found")
255	  if (!ext.officialTier) throw new ApprovalError("extension_not_official")
256	
257	  const revokedAt = new Date()
258	  const affected = await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
259	    return approvalsRepo.applyRevocation(tx, params.extensionId, {
260	      revokedAt,
261	      revokedByUserId: params.superAdminUserId,
262	      revocationNote: params.note,
263	    })
264	  })
265	  if (affected === 0) throw new ApprovalError("extension_not_official")
266	
267	  // Best-effort notification after commit; see submitRequest for rationale.
268	  await safeSend({
269	    name: "extension/tier.revoked",
270	    data: {
271	      extensionId: params.extensionId,
272	      revokedByUserId: params.superAdminUserId,
273	      revokedAt: revokedAt.toISOString(),
274	      note: params.note,
275	    },
276	  })
277	
278	  return { extensionId: params.extensionId, revokedAt }
279	}
280	
281	export interface ReviewerQueueFilters {
282	  tier?: OfficialTier
283	  subCat?: string
284	  productLineId?: string
285	}
286	
287	// Reviewer queue — pending requests in any cell this user covers. Returns
288	// an empty array when the user has no cell assignments (and is not a
289	// super-admin); super-admins see the whole pending queue.
290	//
291	// Optional `filters` narrow the queue without changing the trust model:
292	// the cells are computed first (so a reviewer never queries outside their
293	// own assignment), then filtered, then handed to listPendingForCells. A
294	// filter that excludes every cell yields an empty `cells` array, which
295	// listPendingForCells short-circuits to [] — no special-case branch.
296	export async function listReviewerQueue(
297	  userId: string,
298	  filters: ReviewerQueueFilters = {},
299	): Promise<approvalsRepo.ApprovalRequestRow[]> {
300	  const db = useDb()
301	  let cells: Array<{
302	    tier: OfficialTier
303	    subCat: string
304	    productLineId: string | null
305	  }>
306	  if (await reviewersRepo.isSuperAdmin(db, userId)) {
307	    // Treat super-admin as "assigned to every cell". Pulled from the matrix
308	    // verbatim so an empty matrix still surfaces no rows — better than a
309	    // silent firehose. Productline IS NULL is encoded as the literal '∅' in
310	    // the dedup key so a (company, cloud, null) cell doesn't collapse onto
311	    // (productLine, cloud, '') by accident.
312	    cells = await reviewersRepo
313	      .listMatrix(db)
314	      .then((rows) =>
315	        Array.from(
316	          new Set(
317	            rows.map((r) => `${r.tier}:${r.subCat}:${r.productLineId ?? "∅"}`),
318	          ),
319	        ).map((k) => {
320	          const [tier, subCat, pl] = k.split(":") as [OfficialTier, string, string]
321	          return {
322	            tier,
323	            subCat,
324	            productLineId: pl === "∅" ? null : pl,
325	          }
326	        }),
327	      )
328	  } else {
329	    cells = await reviewersRepo.listCellsForUser(db, userId)
330	  }
331	
332	  const narrowed = cells.filter((c) => {
333	    if (filters.tier && c.tier !== filters.tier) return false
334	    if (filters.subCat && c.subCat !== filters.subCat) return false
335	    if (filters.productLineId && c.productLineId !== filters.productLineId)
336	      return false
337	    return true
338	  })
339	  return approvalsRepo.listPendingForCells(db, narrowed)
340	}
341	
342	export async function listPublisherRequests(
343	  userId: string,
344	): Promise<approvalsRepo.ApprovalRequestRow[]> {
345	  return approvalsRepo.listForPublisher(useDb(), userId)
346	}
347

1	import { and, desc, eq, inArray, isNull, sql } from "drizzle-orm"
2	
3	import type {
4	  ApprovalStatus,
5	  OfficialTier,
6	} from "~~/shared/approvals/state"
7	import {
8	  approvalRequests,
9	  approvalReviewers,
10	  extensions,
11	} from "~~/shared/db/schema"
12	
13	import type { Transactable } from "./types"
14	
15	// `approval_requests` table accessor. Used by the approval orchestrator to
16	// drive the publisher submit / reviewer decide / publisher withdraw flow.
17	// `ApprovalStatus` and `OfficialTier` are re-exported so callers that
18	// already depend on the repository module don't need a second import.
19	
20	export type { ApprovalStatus, OfficialTier }
21	
22	export interface ApprovalRequestRow {
23	  id: string
24	  extensionId: string
25	  requestedTier: OfficialTier
26	  subCat: string
27	  productLineId: string | null
28	  requestedByUserId: string
29	  reason: string | null
30	  status: ApprovalStatus
31	  decidedByUserId: string | null
32	  decidedAt: Date | null
33	  reviewerNote: string | null
34	  createdAt: Date
35	  updatedAt: Date
36	}
37	
38	const fullSelect = {
39	  id: approvalRequests.id,
40	  extensionId: approvalRequests.extensionId,
41	  requestedTier: approvalRequests.requestedTier,
42	  subCat: approvalRequests.subCat,
43	  productLineId: approvalRequests.productLineId,
44	  requestedByUserId: approvalRequests.requestedByUserId,
45	  reason: approvalRequests.reason,
46	  status: approvalRequests.status,
47	  decidedByUserId: approvalRequests.decidedByUserId,
48	  decidedAt: approvalRequests.decidedAt,
49	  reviewerNote: approvalRequests.reviewerNote,
50	  createdAt: approvalRequests.createdAt,
51	  updatedAt: approvalRequests.updatedAt,
52	}
53	
54	export interface InsertApprovalRequest {
55	  id: string
56	  extensionId: string
57	  requestedTier: OfficialTier
58	  subCat: string
59	  productLineId: string | null
60	  requestedByUserId: string
61	  reason: string | null
62	}
63	
64	export async function insertRequest(
65	  db: Transactable,
66	  row: InsertApprovalRequest,
67	): Promise<ApprovalRequestRow> {
68	  const [created] = await db
69	    .insert(approvalRequests)
70	    .values(row)
71	    .returning(fullSelect)
72	  if (!created) throw new Error("approval request insert vanished")
73	  return created
74	}
75	
76	export async function findById(
77	  db: Transactable,
78	  id: string,
79	): Promise<ApprovalRequestRow | null> {
80	  const [row] = await db
81	    .select(fullSelect)
82	    .from(approvalRequests)
83	    .where(eq(approvalRequests.id, id))
84	    .limit(1)
85	  return row ?? null
86	}
87	
88	// Bulk variant of `findPendingByExtension` for the publisher dashboard —
89	// the listing already loads many extensions, so we fan in a single query
90	// rather than N round-trips and return a Map<extId, pendingRequest>.
91	export async function findPendingForExtensions(
92	  db: Transactable,
93	  extensionIds: string[],
94	): Promise<Map<string, ApprovalRequestRow>> {
95	  if (extensionIds.length === 0) return new Map()
96	  const rows = await db
97	    .select(fullSelect)
98	    .from(approvalRequests)
99	    .where(
100	      and(
101	        inArray(approvalRequests.extensionId, extensionIds),
102	        eq(approvalRequests.status, "pending"),
103	      ),
104	    )
105	  return new Map(rows.map((r) => [r.extensionId, r]))
106	}
107	
108	// Used by the at-most-one-pending guard in the submit orchestrator.
109	export async function findPendingByExtension(
110	  db: Transactable,
111	  extensionId: string,
112	): Promise<ApprovalRequestRow | null> {
113	  const [row] = await db
114	    .select(fullSelect)
115	    .from(approvalRequests)
116	    .where(
117	      and(
118	        eq(approvalRequests.extensionId, extensionId),
119	        eq(approvalRequests.status, "pending"),
120	      ),
121	    )
122	    .limit(1)
123	  return row ?? null
124	}
125	
126	// Publisher's "my requests" view.
127	export async function listForPublisher(
128	  db: Transactable,
129	  userId: string,
130	): Promise<ApprovalRequestRow[]> {
131	  return db
132	    .select(fullSelect)
133	    .from(approvalRequests)
134	    .where(eq(approvalRequests.requestedByUserId, userId))
135	    .orderBy(desc(approvalRequests.createdAt))
136	}
137	
138	// Reviewer's queue — pending requests in any (tier, subCat, productLineId?)
139	// cell that the reviewer is assigned to. Implemented as a single query
140	// against the cells the reviewer covers; the orchestrator pre-computes the
141	// cell list from `approval_reviewers` to keep this method driver-portable
142	// (no LATERAL). ProductLineId is matched IS NULL for company-tier cells
143	// and = productLineId for productLine-tier cells, so a company-tier
144	// reviewer never sees productLine-tier requests and vice versa.
145	export async function listPendingForCells(
146	  db: Transactable,
147	  cells: Array<{ tier: OfficialTier; subCat: string; productLineId: string | null }>,
148	): Promise<ApprovalRequestRow[]> {
149	  if (cells.length === 0) return []
150	  const cellMatches = cells.map((c) =>
151	    and(
152	      eq(approvalRequests.requestedTier, c.tier),
153	      eq(approvalRequests.subCat, c.subCat),
154	      c.productLineId === null
155	        ? isNull(approvalRequests.productLineId)
156	        : eq(approvalRequests.productLineId, c.productLineId),
157	    ),
158	  )
159	  return db
160	    .select(fullSelect)
161	    .from(approvalRequests)
162	    .where(
163	      and(
164	        eq(approvalRequests.status, "pending"),
165	        sql`(${sql.join(cellMatches, sql` OR `)})`,
166	      ),
167	    )
168	    .orderBy(desc(approvalRequests.createdAt))
169	}
170	
171	export interface DecidePatch {
172	  status: "approved" | "rejected"
173	  decidedByUserId: string
174	  decidedAt: Date
175	  reviewerNote: string | null
176	}
177	
178	// Optimistic-locking variant: only updates a request that's still pending.
179	// Returns the number of affected rows so the orchestrator can detect a
180	// race (a second reviewer that committed between the orchestrator's
181	// pre-check and this UPDATE) and surface it as `request_not_pending`.
182	export async function applyDecision(
183	  db: Transactable,
184	  id: string,
185	  patch: DecidePatch,
186	): Promise<number> {
187	  const updated = await db
188	    .update(approvalRequests)
189	    .set({ ...patch, updatedAt: new Date() })
190	    .where(
191	      and(
192	        eq(approvalRequests.id, id),
193	        eq(approvalRequests.status, "pending"),
194	      ),
195	    )
196	    .returning({ id: approvalRequests.id })
197	  return updated.length
198	}
199	
200	// Same optimistic-locking shape as `applyDecision` — only a pending
201	// request can be withdrawn, and the affected row count tells the caller
202	// whether the transition actually fired.
203	export async function applyWithdraw(
204	  db: Transactable,
205	  id: string,
206	  decidedAt: Date,
207	): Promise<number> {
208	  const updated = await db
209	    .update(approvalRequests)
210	    .set({ status: "withdrawn", decidedAt, updatedAt: new Date() })
211	    .where(
212	      and(
213	        eq(approvalRequests.id, id),
214	        eq(approvalRequests.status, "pending"),
215	      ),
216	    )
217	    .returning({ id: approvalRequests.id })
218	  return updated.length
219	}
220	
221	// Companion mutator for the approve path — stamps `officialTier` and the
222	// matching `productLineId` (null for company tier) on the parent extension.
223	// Lives here (rather than in `extensions.ts`) because the approval
224	// orchestrator is the only legitimate writer; keeping the call adjacent to
225	// the request mutation makes that intent obvious. The DB CHECK enforces
226	// productLineId is non-null iff tier='productLine', so a bad pair from a
227	// caller is rejected at the boundary. Also clears the revocation audit
228	// fields in the same UPDATE — the invariant is "you're either currently
229	// Official with no pending revocation explanation, or Unofficial".
230	export async function setExtensionOfficialTier(
231	  db: Transactable,
232	  extensionId: string,
233	  tier: OfficialTier,
234	  productLineId: string | null,
235	): Promise<void> {
236	  await db
237	    .update(extensions)
238	    .set({
239	      officialTier: tier,
240	      productLineId,
241	      revokedAt: null,
242	      revokedByUserId: null,
243	      revocationNote: null,
244	      updatedAt: new Date(),
245	    })
246	    .where(eq(extensions.id, extensionId))
247	}
248	
249	// Companion mutator for the revoke path — atomically clears
250	// `officialTier` and `productLineId` while writing the audit trio
251	// (`revokedAt`, `revokedByUserId`, `revocationNote`). The orchestrator's
252	// pre-check already confirmed the row was Official; this method's
253	// returned affected-row count is the race-safe equivalent of a CAS,
254	// surfacing "someone else already revoked / re-approved" as a 0.
255	export interface RevocationPatch {
256	  revokedAt: Date
257	  revokedByUserId: string
258	  revocationNote: string
259	}
260	export async function applyRevocation(
261	  db: Transactable,
262	  extensionId: string,
263	  patch: RevocationPatch,
264	): Promise<number> {
265	  const updated = await db
266	    .update(extensions)
267	    .set({
268	      officialTier: null,
269	      productLineId: null,
270	      revokedAt: patch.revokedAt,
271	      revokedByUserId: patch.revokedByUserId,
272	      revocationNote: patch.revocationNote,
273	      updatedAt: new Date(),
274	    })
275	    .where(
276	      and(
277	        eq(extensions.id, extensionId),
278	        sql`${extensions.officialTier} IS NOT NULL`,
279	      ),
280	    )
281	    .returning({ id: extensions.id })
282	  return updated.length
283	}
284	
285	// Bulk lookup of `officialTier` for a set of extensions — used by the
286	// publisher dashboard to render the per-row badge alongside any pending
287	// requests in one round-trip.
288	export async function findTiersForExtensions(
289	  db: Transactable,
290	  extensionIds: string[],
291	): Promise<Map<string, OfficialTier | null>> {
292	  if (extensionIds.length === 0) return new Map()
293	  const rows = await db
294	    .select({ id: extensions.id, officialTier: extensions.officialTier })
295	    .from(extensions)
296	    .where(inArray(extensions.id, extensionIds))
297	  return new Map(rows.map((r) => [r.id, r.officialTier ?? null]))
298	}
299	
300	// Used by guards in `decide.post.ts` — confirms a user is assigned to the
301	// cell that the request originated from. Super-admins are handled in the
302	// orchestrator (a separate join) so this stays a pure reviewer check.
303	// ProductLineId is null for company-tier requests and the cell match must
304	// be exact on that NULLness to keep tiers from leaking into one another.
305	export async function isReviewerForCell(
306	  db: Transactable,
307	  userId: string,
308	  tier: OfficialTier,
309	  subCat: string,
310	  productLineId: string | null,
311	): Promise<boolean> {
312	  const [row] = await db
313	    .select({ id: approvalReviewers.id })
314	    .from(approvalReviewers)
315	    .where(
316	      and(
317	        eq(approvalReviewers.userId, userId),
318	        eq(approvalReviewers.tier, tier),
319	        eq(approvalReviewers.subCat, subCat),
320	        productLineId === null
321	          ? isNull(approvalReviewers.productLineId)
322	          : eq(approvalReviewers.productLineId, productLineId),
323	      ),
324	    )
325	    .limit(1)
326	  return !!row
327	}
328

1	import {
2	  isSuperAdmin,
3	  listCompanySubCatsForUser,
4	  listMatrix,
5	} from "~~/server/repositories/reviewers"
6	import { listAllProductLines } from "~~/server/repositories/productLines"
7	
8	// Returns the reviewer matrix plus per-cell `canEdit` annotations and the
9	// productLine list, so the admin UI can render the productLine grid and
10	// grey non-editable cells in one round-trip. The access gate widens from
11	// "super-admin only" to "super-admin OR any company-tier reviewer" — the
12	// non-super viewer sees the full read-only matrix and can only mutate
13	// productLine cells in their own subCats (see requireCellAdmin).
14	export default defineEventHandler(async (event) => {
15	  const user = await requireUser(event)
16	  const db = useDb()
17	  const [superAdmin, companySubCats] = await Promise.all([
18	    isSuperAdmin(db, user.id),
19	    listCompanySubCatsForUser(db, user.id),
20	  ])
21	  const isCompanyAdmin = companySubCats.length > 0
22	  if (!superAdmin && !isCompanyAdmin) {
23	    throw createError({ statusCode: 403, statusMessage: "Forbidden" })
24	  }
25	
26	  const editableSubCats = new Set(companySubCats)
27	  const [reviewers, productLines] = await Promise.all([
28	    listMatrix(db),
29	    listAllProductLines(db),
30	  ])
31	
32	  return {
33	    ok: true,
34	    productLines,
35	    reviewers: reviewers.map((r) => ({
36	      ...r,
37	      canEdit:
38	        superAdmin ||
39	        (r.tier === "productLine" && editableSubCats.has(r.subCat)),
40	    })),
41	    viewer: {
42	      isSuperAdmin: superAdmin,
43	      companySubCats,
44	    },
45	  }
46	})
47

1	import { insertReviewer } from "~~/server/repositories/reviewers"
2	import { AssignReviewerSchema } from "~~/shared/validators/approvals"
3	
4	// Per-cell authorisation: super-admins anywhere, company admins of subCat X
5	// may assign productLine reviewers for subCat X (any productLine), and
6	// company-tier cells stay super-admin-only. The validator's iff-rule
7	// already guarantees productLineId is present iff tier='productLine'.
8	export default defineEventHandler(async (event) => {
9	  const body = await readValidatedBody(event, (raw) =>
10	    AssignReviewerSchema.parse(raw),
11	  )
12	  await requireCellAdmin(event, {
13	    tier: body.tier,
14	    subCat: body.subCat,
15	    productLineId: body.productLineId ?? null,
16	  })
17	
18	  await insertReviewer(useDb(), {
19	    id: crypto.randomUUID(),
20	    tier: body.tier,
21	    subCat: body.subCat,
22	    productLineId: body.productLineId ?? null,
23	    userId: body.userId,
24	  })
25	  return { ok: true }
26	})
27

1	import {
2	  deleteReviewer,
3	  findReviewerById,
4	} from "~~/server/repositories/reviewers"
5	import { UnassignReviewerSchema } from "~~/shared/validators/approvals"
6	
7	// Load the row first so the cell-aware gate authorises against the row's
8	// own (tier, subCat, productLineId), not against anything the caller
9	// supplies. Without this, a company admin of subCat X could uncover the
10	// id of a company-tier reviewer in any subCat by guessing — the request
11	// body alone can't tell us which cell the id belongs to.
12	export default defineEventHandler(async (event) => {
13	  const body = await readValidatedBody(event, (raw) =>
14	    UnassignReviewerSchema.parse(raw),
15	  )
16	  const row = await findReviewerById(useDb(), body.id)
17	  if (!row) {
18	    throw createError({ statusCode: 404, statusMessage: "reviewer_not_found" })
19	  }
20	  await requireCellAdmin(event, {
21	    tier: row.tier,
22	    subCat: row.subCat,
23	    productLineId: row.productLineId,
24	  })
25	  await deleteReviewer(useDb(), body.id)
26	  return { ok: true }
27	})
28

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/validators/approvals.ts",
  "content": "import { z } from \"zod\"\n\nimport {\n  ALL_L1_KEYS,\n  ALL_L2_KEYS,\n  type CategoryLevel,\n} from \"~~/shared/taxonomy\"\n\nconst L1_KEY_SET = new Set<string>(ALL_L1_KEYS)\nconst L2_KEY_SET = new Set<string>(ALL_L2_KEYS)\n\n// `SUB_CAT_KEY_LIST` is the source of truth for the publisher subCat picker\n// and the request-row validator — same set as `ALL_L1_KEYS`, just re-exported\n// under the historical name so the matrix UI and the publish dialog stay\n// in lockstep.\nexport const SUB_CAT_KEY_LIST: readonly string[] = ALL_L1_KEYS\n\nexport const OfficialTier = z.enum([\"productLine\", \"company\"])\nexport type OfficialTier = z.infer<typeof OfficialTier>\n\nexport const SubCatKey = z.string().refine((v) => L1_KEY_SET.has(v), {\n  message: \"must be a key from FUNC_TAXONOMY\",\n})\n\nexport const ExtensionCategory = z.enum([\"skills\", \"mcp\", \"slash\", \"plugins\"])\nexport type ExtensionCategory = z.infer<typeof ExtensionCategory>\n\nexport const AdminCategoryLevel = z.enum([\"all\", \"macro\", \"micro\"])\nexport type AdminCategoryLevel = CategoryLevel\n\n// Product-line ids are seeded text PKs in kebab-case (wireless, datacom,\n// terminals, cloud as of 0009). Validate the shape here; the DB FK + CHECK\n// constraint enforces membership and the iff-rule against the actual tier.\nexport const ProductLineId = z\n  .string()\n  .trim()\n  .min(1)\n  .max(40)\n  .regex(/^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$/, {\n    message: \"must be a kebab-case product-line id\",\n  })\nexport type ProductLineId = z.infer<typeof ProductLineId>\n\n// iff-rule helpers — the DB CHECK is the source of truth, but surfacing\n// the rule at the validator layer turns a bad request into a typed Zod\n// error instead of a generic 23514 from Postgres.\nconst requireProductLineIff = <T extends {\n  tier?: OfficialTier\n  requestedTier?: OfficialTier\n  productLineId?: string | undefined\n}>(\n  v: T,\n  tierKey: \"tier\" | \"requestedTier\",\n): boolean => {\n  const tier = v[tierKey]\n  const hasPl = !!v.productLineId\n  if (tier === \"productLine\") return hasPl\n  if (tier === \"company\") return !hasPl\n  return true\n}\n\nexport const APPROVAL_REASON_MAX = 500\nexport const APPROVAL_NOTE_MAX = 500\nexport const REVOKE_NOTE_MAX = 500\n\nconst optionalText = (max: number) =>\n  z\n    .string()\n    .trim()\n    .max(max)\n    .optional()\n    .transform((v) => (v && v.length > 0 ? v : undefined))\n\nexport const SubmitApprovalSchema = z\n  .object({\n    extensionId: z.string().trim().min(1),\n    requestedTier: OfficialTier,\n    subCat: SubCatKey,\n    productLineId: ProductLineId.optional(),\n    reason: optionalText(APPROVAL_REASON_MAX),\n  })\n  .refine((v) => requireProductLineIff(v, \"requestedTier\"), {\n    path: [\"productLineId\"],\n    message: \"productLineId is required iff requestedTier=productLine\",\n  })\nexport type SubmitApprovalInput = z.infer<typeof SubmitApprovalSchema>\n\n// Discriminated union: a reviewer note only makes sense on a rejection.\n// The orchestrator drops any note sent with an approve action anyway —\n// making that explicit at the API boundary catches the mistake before\n// it crosses the wire instead of silently after.\nexport const DecideApprovalSchema = z.discriminatedUnion(\"decision\", [\n  z.object({\n    requestId: z.string().trim().min(1),\n    decision: z.literal(\"approve\"),\n  }),\n  z.object({\n    requestId: z.string().trim().min(1),\n    decision: z.literal(\"reject\"),\n    note: optionalText(APPROVAL_NOTE_MAX),\n  }),\n])\nexport type DecideApprovalInput = z.infer<typeof DecideApprovalSchema>\n\nexport const WithdrawApprovalSchema = z.object({\n  requestId: z.string().trim().min(1),\n})\nexport type WithdrawApprovalInput = z.infer<typeof WithdrawApprovalSchema>\n\n// Super-admin Revoke action from the detail page. The note is REQUIRED\n// (mirrors the existing reject flow) — the publisher dashboard surfaces\n// it directly so a missing note would leave the publisher in the dark.\n// `.trim().min(1)` enforces \"non-empty after trim\" so whitespace-only\n// submissions are caught at the validator boundary.\nexport const RevokeTierSchema = z.object({\n  extensionId: z.string().trim().min(1),\n  note: z.string().trim().min(1).max(REVOKE_NOTE_MAX),\n})\nexport type RevokeTierInput = z.infer<typeof RevokeTierSchema>\n\n// Matrix admin assignment — five coordinates. Replaces the 3-coord\n// pre-redesign shape. Refines on top of the level/key keyspace and the\n// productLineId iff-rule:\n//   - level='all' → key='*'\n//   - level='macro' → key is an l1 leaf\n//   - level='micro' → key is an l2 leaf\n//   - productLineId is required iff tier='productLine'\n// Both refines surface as Zod errors so the endpoint can render a useful\n// message instead of bouncing the raw 23514 from Postgres.\nexport const AssignAdminSchema = z\n  .object({\n    extensionCategory: ExtensionCategory,\n    tier: OfficialTier,\n    productLineId: ProductLineId.optional(),\n    categoryLevel: AdminCategoryLevel,\n    categoryKey: z.string().trim().min(1).max(40),\n    userId: z.string().trim().min(1),\n  })\n  .refine((v) => requireProductLineIff(v, \"tier\"), {\n    path: [\"productLineId\"],\n    message: \"productLineId is required iff tier=productLine\",\n  })\n  .refine(\n    (v) => {\n      if (v.categoryLevel === \"all\") return v.categoryKey === \"*\"\n      if (v.categoryLevel === \"macro\") return L1_KEY_SET.has(v.categoryKey)\n      return L2_KEY_SET.has(v.categoryKey)\n    },\n    {\n      path: [\"categoryKey\"],\n      message:\n        \"categoryKey must be '*' for level=all, an l1 key for level=macro, or an l2 key for level=micro\",\n    },\n  )\nexport type AssignAdminInput = z.infer<typeof AssignAdminSchema>\n\nexport const UnassignAdminSchema = z.object({\n  id: z.string().trim().min(1),\n})\nexport type UnassignAdminInput = z.infer<typeof UnassignAdminSchema>\n\nexport const ListApprovalsQuerySchema = z.object({\n  // \"mine\"  → requests this user submitted as a publisher\n  // \"queue\" → pending requests routed to this user as a reviewer\n  view: z.enum([\"mine\", \"queue\"]).default(\"mine\"),\n  // Reviewer-queue narrowing keys. Reused from the cell-shape validators\n  // above so the queue picker stays in lockstep with the publisher dialog\n  // and the listing rail.\n  tier: OfficialTier.optional(),\n  subCat: SubCatKey.optional(),\n  productLineId: ProductLineId.optional(),\n  extensionCategory: ExtensionCategory.optional(),\n})\nexport type ListApprovalsQuery = z.infer<typeof ListApprovalsQuerySchema>\n"
}
